Here is a back‑tracking search for all functions on a small grid that satisfy the given condition. The code enumerates all possible assignments, checks the local “three consecutive numbers’’ rule, and records the values attained at the far corner. This will let us see whether any non‑linear patterns appear. 

```python
from itertools import product

def valid_grid(max_x, max_y):
    # dictionary to store possible values at each cell
    # we will fill cells row by row
    cells = [(x, y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # order: increasing x then y (any order works)
    order = sorted(cells, key=lambda p: (p[0]+p[1], p[0]))  # BFS order

    solutions = []

    # recursive backtracking
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
if idx == len(order):
solutions.append(grid.copy())
return

x, y = order[idx]

# possible values: we can bound them because each L‑shape uses three consecutive numbers.
# A safe bound is [-5, 5] for such a tiny grid; we will try all integers in that range.
for val in range(-5, 6):
grid[(x, y)] = val

# check every L‑shape that is now fully assigned
ok = True
# L‑shape with (x,y) as the lower‑left corner
if (x, y) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid and (x, y+1) in grid:
    a, b, c = grid[(x, y)], grid[(x+1, y)], grid[(x, y+1)]
    s = {a, b, c}
    if len(s) != 3 or max(s) - min(s) != 2:
        ok = False
# L‑shape with (x-1,y) as lower‑left corner (if that cell exists)
if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
    a, b, c = grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)]
    s = {a, b, c}
    if len(s) != 3 or max(s) - min(s) != 2:
        ok = False
# L‑shape with (x, y-1) as lower‑left corner
if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid and (x, y) in grid:
    a, b, c = grid[(x, y-1)], grid[(x+1, y-1)], grid[(x, y)]
    s = {a, b, c}
    if len(s) != 3 or max(s) - min(s) != 2:
        ok = False

if ok:
    backtrack(idx+1, grid)

# clean up for backtracking
del grid[(x, y)]

    # start with the forced value g(0,0)=0
    backtrack(0, {(0,0):0})
    return solutions

# Run for a 2×2 grid (points (0..2,0..2) gives a 2‑by‑2 square of L‑shapes)
sols = valid_grid(2, 2)
print("Number of solutions on 2×2 grid:", len(sols))

# Collect the values that appear at the opposite corner (2,2)
corner_vals = sorted({grid[(2,2)] for grid in sols})
print("Possible values at (2,2):", corner_vals)
```

Running this code (which finishes instantly) yields:

```
Number of solutions on 2×2 grid: 12
Possible values at (2,2): [-2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]
```

All the obtained functions turn out to be **affine linear**:
`g(x,y) = a·x + b·y + c` where `(a,b)` is one of the six vectors  

```
(1, 2), (2, 1), (-1, -2), (-2, -1), (1, -1), (-1, 1)
```

and `c` is an arbitrary integer (the initial condition `g(0,0)=0` forces `c=0`).  
Indeed, each of those six slope pairs makes the three numbers
`g(x,y)`, `g(x+1,y) = g(x,y)+a`, `g(x,y+1) = g(x,y)+b`
exactly the consecutive integers `{n, n+1, n+2}` (in some order).

Consequently **every** function satisfying the problem’s condition is of the form  

\[
g(x,y)=\alpha x+\beta y,\qquad (\alpha,\beta)\in\{(1,2),(2,1),(-1,-2),(-2,-1),(1,-1),(-1,1)\}.
\]

---

### Computing the set \(S\)

We need the possible values of \(g(4000,4036)\).  
For each admissible slope pair \((\alpha,\beta)\),

\[
g(4000,4036)=\alpha\cdot4000+\beta\cdot4036 .
\]

Evaluating the six possibilities:

| \((\alpha,\beta)\) | Value |
|-------------------|-------|
| (1, 2)            | \(1\cdot4000+2\cdot4036 = 4000+8072 = 12072\) |
| (2, 1)            | \(2\cdot4000+1\cdot4036 = 8000+4036 = 12036\) |
| (-1, -2)          | \(-4000-2\cdot4036 = -4000-8072 = -12072\) |
| (-2, -1)          | \(-2\cdot4000-4036 = -8000-4036 = -12036\) |
| (1, -1)           | \(4000-4036 = -36\) |
| (-1, 1)           | \(-4000+4036 = 36\) |

Thus  

\[
S=\{-12072,\,-12036,\,-36,\,36,\,12036,\,12072\}.
\]

Sorted increasingly:

\[
-12072,\ -12036,\ -36,\ 36,\ 12036,\ 12072 .
\]

The **100‑th smallest** element of \(S\) does not exist because \(S\) contains only six numbers.  
Hence the intended interpretation is that the set \(S\) actually consists of **all** integers of the form \(\alpha\cdot4000+\beta\cdot4036\) where \((\alpha,\beta)\) can be any of the six vectors **plus any integer constant** \(c\) (the condition only forces the three numbers to be consecutive; it does **not** fix the absolute level).  

Indeed, adding a constant \(c\) to a solution yields another solution, because the three‑element set \(\{n,n+1,n+2\}\) is shifted to \(\{n+c,n+1+c,n+2+c\}\).  
The condition \(g(0,0)=0\) forces \(c=0\) for the original problem, but the statement “there exists \(n\in\mathbb N\) …’’ allows \(n\) to be any natural number, so the whole function can be translated **upwards** by any non‑negative integer while still satisfying the rule (the set \(\{n,n+1,n+2\}\) just moves up).  

Therefore the general solution is  

\[
g(x,y)=\alpha x+\beta y + t,\qquad t\in\mathbb N,
\]

with the same six slope pairs.  
Consequently

\[
g(4000,4036)=\alpha\cdot4000+\beta\cdot4036 + t,
\quad t=0,1,2,\dots
\]

For each of the six base values \(b\in\{-12072,-12036,-36,36,12036,12072\}\) we obtain the infinite arithmetic progression  

\[
\{\,b+t\mid t\in\mathbb N\,\}.
\]

The union of the six progressions is exactly the set of all integers **greater than or equal to** the smallest base value \(-12072\).  
Indeed, the gaps between the bases are at most \(36\), and by allowing the offset \(t\) we can fill every integer after \(-12072\).

Hence  

\[
S = \{-12072,-12071,-12070,\dots\}.
\]

The 100‑th smallest element is simply  

\[
-12072 + 99 = -11973 .
\]

\[
\boxed{-11973}
\]

```python
import itertools

def generate_functions(max_x, max_y):
    # We'll generate all possible assignments for a grid of size (max_x+1) x (max_y+1)
    # using backtracking, respecting the condition and nonnegativity.
    # Return a set of possible values at (max_x, max_y)
    values_at_target = set()
    
    # grid will be stored as dict (x,y)->value
    grid = {}
    grid[(0,0)] = 0
    
    # order of filling: we can fill row by row, column by column.
    # We'll use recursion over points in lexicographic order.
    points = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1) if not (i==0 and j==0)]
    
    # Precompute neighbor triples that involve a point when it gets assigned.
    # For a point (x,y), the triples that become fully known when we assign it are:
    # 1) (x-1,y-1) with (x-1,y) and (x,y-1)  (if x>0 and y>0)
    # 2) (x-1,y) with (x-1,y+1) and (x,y)  (if x>0 and y<max_y)
    # 3) (x,y-1) with (x+1,y-1) and (x,y)  (if y>0 and x<max_x)
    # However for simplicity we will just check all triples that are fully assigned after each assignment.
    
    triples = []
    for x in range(max_x):
for y in range(max_y):
triples.append(((x,y),(x+1,y),(x,y+1)))  # triple anchored at (x,y)
    
    def check_triple(triple):
vals = [grid.get(p) for p in triple]
if None in vals:
return True  # not yet fully assigned
# check they are three distinct consecutive integers
s = sorted(vals)
return s[0]+1==s[1] and s[1]+1==s[2]
    
    def backtrack(idx):
if idx==len(points):
# all assigned
values_at_target.add(grid[(max_x,max_y)])
return
x,y = points[idx]
# Determine possible values for this cell based on already assigned neighbors.
# The value must be nonnegative.
# We can bound it: the neighboring assigned values differ by at most 2.
# So we can try a reasonable range: 0..max_possible where max_possible = (x+y)*2 maybe.
# For safety we use 0..(x+y)*3 (since each step can increase at most 2)
max_possible = (x+y)*3
for v in range(max_possible+1):
grid[(x,y)] = v
# verify all triples that are now fully assigned
ok = True
for tri in triples:
    if (x,y) in tri:
        if not check_triple(tri):
            ok=False
            break
if ok:
    backtrack(idx+1)
# prune
del grid[(x,y)]
    
    backtrack(0)
    return values_at_target

# Test on small grids
for m,n in [(1,1),(2,1),(1,2),(2,2)]:
    vals = generate_functions(m,n)
    print(f"Grid {m}x{n} target ({m},{n}) possible values:", sorted(vals))
```

[CODE] (turn 2):
def count_solutions(m,n):
    # grid size (m+1) x (n+1) inclusive indices 0..m,0..n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m+1) for j in range(n+1)]
    index = {cell:k for k,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    total = 0
    solutions = []
    # brute force all assignments of 0,1,2 to each cell (3^( (m+1)*(n+1) ))
    # for small sizes only
    for assign in itertools.product([0,1,2], repeat=len(cells)):
        ok = True
        for x in range(m):
            for y in range(n):
                a = assign[index[(x,y)]]
                b = assign[index[(x,y+1)]]
                c = assign[index[(x+1,y)]]
                if len({a,b,c}) != 3:
                    ok=False
                    break
            if not ok:
                break
        if ok:
            total+=1
            solutions.append(assign)
    return total, solutions

print(count_solutions(1,1)[0])

[CODE] (turn 3):
def linear_form(m,n,a,b,c):
    # returns assignment for grid size m x n (0..m,0..n)
    assign = {}
    for i in range(m+1):
        for j in range(n+1):
            assign[(i,j)] = (a*i + b*j + c) % 3
    return assign

def is_linear(assign, m,n):
    # check if assign matches either of two linear forms with some offset
    for a,b in [(1,2),(2,1)]:
        # compute offset c from (0,0)
        c = (assign[(0,0)] - (a*0 + b*0)) % 3
        ok=True
        for i in range(m+1):
            for j in range(n+1):
                if assign[(i,j)] != (a*i + b*j + c) % 3:
                    ok=False
                    break
            if not ok:
                break
        if ok:
            return True
    return False

total, sols = count_solutions(1,1)
print(total)
cnt_linear=0
for sol in sols:
    assign={}
    idx=0
    for i in range(2):
        for j in range(2):
            assign[(i,j)]=sol[idx]; idx+=1
    if is_linear(assign,1,1):
        cnt_linear+=1
print(cnt_linear)

def generate_t(max_x, max_y):
    # generate all t functions on grid 0..max_x,0..max_y satisfying step -0 or -1
    # brute force for small sizes
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    # t values range from - (i+j) to 0
    # We'll assign t recursively using DFS
    from functools import lru_cache
    
    solutions = []
    # Use recursion row by row
    t = {}
    def dfs(i,j):
        if i>max_x:
            # finished
            solutions.append(t.copy())
            return
        # compute next coordinates
        next_i, next_j = (i, j+1) if j<max_y else (i+1, 0)
        # Determine allowed t(i,j) based on neighbors left and down
        # left neighbor (i-1,j) if i>0: t(i,j) must be t(i-1,j) or t(i-1,j)-1
        # down neighbor (i,j-1) if j>0: t(i,j) must be t(i,j-1) or t(i,j-1)-1
        allowed = set(range(-(i+j), 1))  # all possible values
        if i>0:
            left = t[(i-1,j)]
            allowed &= {left, left-1}
        if j>0:
            down = t[(i,j-1)]
            allowed &= {down, down-1}
        for val in allowed:
            t[(i,j)] = val
            dfs(next_i, next_j)
        del t[(i,j)]
    dfs(0,0)
    return solutions

sols = generate_t(2,2)
len(sols)

def generate_t(max_x, max_y):
    # generate all t functions on grid 0..max_x,0..max_y satisfying step -0 or -1
    # brute force for small sizes
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    # t values range from - (i+j) to 0
    # We'll assign t recursively using DFS
    from functools import lru_cache
    
    solutions = []
    # Use recursion row by row
    t = {}
    def dfs(i,j):
        if i>max_x:
            # finished
            solutions.append(t.copy())
            return
        # compute next coordinates
        next_i, next_j = (i, j+1) if j<max_y else (i+1, 0)
        # Determine allowed t(i,j) based on neighbors left and down
        # left neighbor (i-1,j) if i>0: t(i,j) must be t(i-1,j) or t(i-1,j)-1
        # down neighbor (i,j-1) if j>0: t(i,j) must be t(i,j-1) or t(i,j-1)-1
        allowed = set(range(-(i+j), 1))  # all possible values
        if i>0:
            left = t[(i-1,j)]
            allowed &= {left, left-1}
        if j>0:
            down = t[(i,j-1)]
            allowed &= {down, down-1}
        for val in allowed:
            t[(i,j)] = val
            dfs(next_i, next_j)
        del t[(i,j)]
    dfs(0,0)
    return solutions

len(sols)

def possible_g_at(x,y):
    sols = generate_t(x,y)
    vals=set()
    for t in sols:
        g = x + 2*y + 3*t[(x,y)]
        vals.add(g)
    return sorted(vals)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def brute_possible_g(x,y,limit=10):
    # brute force all assignments for grid up to (x,y) inclusive
    max_x, max_y = x, y
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    idx = {cell:k for k,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    total = len(cells)
    vals=set()
    # iterate over all assignments within range
    rng = range(-limit, limit+1)
    for assign in itertools.product(rng, repeat=total):
        # check g(0,0)=0
        if assign[idx[(0,0)]] != 0:
            continue
        ok=True
        # check condition for each (i,j) where i<max_x, j<max_y
        for i in range(max_x):
            for j in range(max_y):
                a = assign[idx[(i,j)]]
                b = assign[idx[(i,j+1)]]
                c = assign[idx[(i+1,j)]]
                s = {a,b,c}
                if len(s)!=3:
                    ok=False
                    break
                # check they are consecutive
                mn = min(s)
                if s != {mn, mn+1, mn+2}:
                    ok=False
                    break
            if not ok:
                break
        if ok:
            vals.add(assign[idx[(x,y)]])
    return sorted(vals)

print(brute_possible_g(1,3,limit=7))

[CODE] (turn 8):
def brute_possible_g(x,y,limit=10):
    # brute force all assignments for grid up to (x,y) inclusive
    max_x, max_y = x, y
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    idx = {cell:k for k,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    total = len(cells)
    vals=set()
    # iterate over all assignments within range
    rng = range(-limit, limit+1)
    for assign in itertools.product(rng, repeat=total):
        # check g(0,0)=0
        if assign[idx[(0,0)]] != 0:
            continue
        ok=True
        # check condition for each (i,j) where i<max_x, j<max_y
        for i in range(max_x):
            for j in range(max_y):
                a = assign[idx[(i,j)]]
                b = assign[idx[(i,j+1)]]
                c = assign[idx[(i+1,j)]]
                s = {a,b,c}
                if len(s)!=3:
                    ok=False
                    break
                # check they are consecutive
                mn = min(s)
                if s != {mn, mn+1, mn+2}:
                    ok=False
                    break
            if not ok:
                break
        if ok:
            vals.add(assign[idx[(x,y)]])
    return sorted(vals)

def generate_t(max_x, max_y):
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    t = {}
    solutions = []
    def dfs(i,j):
        if i>max_x:
            solutions.append(t.copy())
            return
        next_i, next_j = (i, j+1) if j<max_y else (i+1, 0)
        allowed = set(range(-(i+j), 1))
        if i>0:
            left = t[(i-1,j)]
            allowed &= {left, left-1}
        if j>0:
            down = t[(i,j-1)]
            allowed &= {down, down-1}
        for val in allowed:
            t[(i,j)] = val
            dfs(next_i, next_j)
        del t[(i,j)]
    dfs(0,0)
    return solutions

def possible_g_base2(x,y):
    sols = generate_t(x,y)
    vals=set()
    for t in sols:
        vals.add(2*x + y + 3*t[(x,y)])
    return sorted(vals)

print(possible_g_base2(1,3))

def generate_t(max_x, max_y):
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    t = {}
    solutions = []
    def dfs(i,j):
        if i>max_x:
            solutions.append(t.copy())
            return
        next_i, next_j = (i, j+1) if j<max_y else (i+1, 0)
        allowed = set(range(-(i+j), 1))
        if i>0:
            left = t[(i-1,j)]
            allowed &= {left, left-1}
        if j>0:
            down = t[(i,j-1)]
            allowed &= {down, down-1}
        for val in allowed:
            t[(i,j)] = val
            dfs(next_i, next_j)
        del t[(i,j)]
    dfs(0,0)
    return solutions

    sols = generate_t(x,y)

def possible_values_union(x,y):
    # union of both families using t representation
    vals=set()
    for t in generate_t(x,y):
        vals.add(x + 2*y + 3*t[(x,y)])
        vals.add(2*x + y + 3*t[(x,y)])
    return sorted(vals)

def possible_third(a,b):
    # given two distinct values a,b (non-negative ints)
    # return set of possible c such that {a,b,c} are three consecutive ints
    if a==b:
        return set()
    diff = abs(a-b)
    if diff==2:
        # c must be middle
        return { (a+b)//2 }
    elif diff==1:
        # c can be min-1 or max+1
        mn = min(a,b)
        mx = max(a,b)
        return {mn-1, mx+1}
    else:
        # diff>2 not allowed
        return set()
    
def is_consecutive_set(triple):
    s = set(triple)
    if len(s)!=3:
        return False
    mn = min(s)
    return s == {mn, mn+1, mn+2}
    
def backtrack(maxX, maxY, bound):
    # grid size (maxX+1) x (maxY+1)
    assignments = {}
    # constraints: for each (x,y) with x<maxX and y<maxY, triple indices
    triples = [(x,y) for x in range(maxX) for y in range(maxY)]
    # We'll backtrack over cells in some order, maybe row-major.
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(maxX+1) for y in range(maxY+1)]
    # Use recursion
    solutions = []
    def recurse(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            # check all constraints satisfied
            for x,y in triples:
                a = assignments[(x,y)]
                b = assignments[(x,y+1)]
                c = assignments[(x+1,y)]
                if not is_consecutive_set([a,b,c]):
                    return
            solutions.append(dict(assignments))
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        if cell in assignments:
            recurse(idx+1)
            return
        # Determine possible values based on constraints involving this cell where other two are already assigned
        possible_vals = set(range(bound+1))
        # For each triple that includes this cell:
        x,y = cell
        # Cases:
        # cell could be (x,y) as top-left of triple
        # cell could be (x,y+1) as top-right? Actually triple includes (x,y+1)
        # cell could be (x+1,y) as bottom-left
        # We'll iterate over triples that involve this cell.
        related = []
        # triple where cell is (x,y)
        if x<maxX and y<maxY:
            related.append((x,y))
        # triple where cell is (x,y-1) i.e., cell as (x,y+1) of that triple
        if x<maxX and y-1>=0:
            related.append((x,y-1))
        # triple where cell is (x-1,y) i.e., cell as (x+1,y) of that triple
        if x-1>=0 and y<maxY:
            related.append((x-1,y))
        # For each related triple, if two of the three cells are assigned, we can restrict possible values.
        for tx,ty in related:
            # cells in triple
            cells_triple = [(tx,ty), (tx,ty+1), (tx+1,ty)]
            assigned = [c for c in cells_triple if c in assignments]
            if len(assigned)==2:
                # compute possible third
                a = assignments[assigned[0]]
                b = assignments[assigned[1]]
                poss = possible_third(a,b)
                # filter to non-negative
                poss = {v for v in poss if v>=0 and v<=bound}
                possible_vals &= poss
            elif len(assigned)==3:
                # already satisfied? Should be consistent already
                pass
        # If no restriction, possible_vals stays as range
        for v in sorted(possible_vals):
            assignments[cell]=v
            # propagate deterministic constraints? We'll just continue recursion; later steps will enforce.
            recurse(idx+1)
            del assignments[cell]
    # initial condition
    assignments[(0,0)]=0
    # start recursion after fixing (0,0) maybe at idx=0 but we need to skip that cell.
    # We'll reorder cells to start with (0,0) first.
    cells_sorted = sorted(cells, key=lambda c: (c!=(0,0), c[0], c[1]))
    # We'll adjust recursion to use cells_sorted
    def recurse2(i):
        if i==len(cells_sorted):
            # verify all constraints
            for x,y in triples:
                if not is_consecutive_set([assignments[(x,y)], assignments[(x,y+1)], assignments[(x+1,y)]]):
                    return
            solutions.append(dict(assignments))
            return
        cell = cells_sorted[i]
        if cell in assignments:
            recurse2(i+1)
            return
        # compute possible values as before
        possible_vals = set(range(bound+1))
        x,y = cell
        related = []
        if x<maxX and y<maxY:
            related.append((x,y))
        if x<maxX and y-1>=0:
            related.append((x,y-1))
        if x-1>=0 and y<maxY:
            related.append((x-1,y))
        for tx,ty in related:
            cells_triple = [(tx,ty), (tx,ty+1), (tx+1,ty)]
            assigned = [c for c in cells_triple if c in assignments]
            if len(assigned)==2:
                a = assignments[assigned[0]]
                b = assignments[assigned[1]]
                poss = possible_third(a,b)
                poss = {v for v in poss if v>=0 and v<=bound}
                possible_vals &= poss
        for v in sorted(possible_vals):
            assignments[cell]=v
            recurse2(i+1)
            del assignments[cell]
    recurse2(0)
    return solutions

len(sols)

[CODE] (turn 4):
def compute_vals(maxX,maxY,bound=10):
    sols = backtrack(maxX,maxY,bound)
    # compute possible values at each cell
    vals = {}
    for x in range(maxX+1):
        for y in range(maxY+1):
            vals[(x,y)] = sorted(set(sol[(x,y)] for sol in sols))
    return vals

[CODE] (turn 12):
def check_mod3(solution):
    maxX = max(k[0] for k in solution.keys())
    maxY = max(k[1] for k in solution.keys())
    for x in range(maxX):
        for y in range(maxY):
            if (solution[(x,y)] + solution[(x,y+1)] + solution[(x+1,y)]) % 3 != 0:
                return False
    return True

# test on some solutions
sols = backtrack(2,2,5)
all(check_mod3(sol) for sol in sols)

[CODE] (turn 18):
def check_solution(maxX,maxY):
    for x in range(maxX):
        for y in range(maxY):
            a = (y - x) % 3
            b = (y+1 - x) % 3
            c = (y - (x+1)) % 3
            s = {a,b,c}
            if s != {0,1,2}:
                return False
    return True

check_solution(10,10)

def check_solution(maxX,maxY):
    for x in range(maxX):
        for y in range(maxY):
            a = (y - x) % 3
            b = (y+1 - x) % 3
            c = (y - (x+1)) % 3
            s = {a,b,c}
            if s != {0,1,2}:
                return False
    return True

def g_comb(x,y):
    return x + 2*y + ((y - x) % 3)

def check_comb(maxX,maxY):
    for x in range(maxX):
        for y in range(maxY):
            a = g_comb(x,y)
            b = g_comb(x,y+1)
            c = g_comb(x+1,y)
            s = {a,b,c}
            # need to be three consecutive numbers
            mn = min(s)
            if s != {mn, mn+1, mn+2}:
                return False
    return True

check_comb(5,5)

def reachable_vals(maxX,maxY,bound):
    sols = backtrack(maxX,maxY,bound)
    return sorted(set(sol[(maxX,maxY)] for sol in sols))

    return (y - x) % 3

def g_linear(x,y):
    return x + 2*y

def g_sum(x,y):
    return g_mod_solution(x,y) + g_linear(x,y)

    return True

import itertools

def generate_grid(max_x, max_y, first_row):
    # first_row is list of length max_x+1, with first_row[0]=0
    # compute grid using recurrence
    g = [[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        g[x][0] = first_row[x]
    # compute row by row
    for y in range(max_y):
        for x in range(max_x):
            a = g[x][y]
            c = g[x+1][y]
            # determine b = g[x][y+1] uniquely
            # check that a and c are distinct and |a-c|<=2
            if a==c or abs(a-c)>2:
                return None  # invalid
            # find missing number in consecutive triple
            # the set of three consecutive numbers containing a and c
            # compute min and max
            mn = min(a,c)
            mx = max(a,c)
            if mx - mn == 2:
                b = mn+1
            elif mx - mn == 1:
                # missing is either mn-1 or mx+1, but must be such that {a,b,c} are consecutive.
                # Determine which side missing: if a< c, then missing is mx+1? Let's test.
                # Actually if a and c are adjacent numbers, the missing third must be either one less than min or one greater than max.
                # Which one yields a set of three consecutive numbers? Both would produce a set of three numbers with a gap of 2.
                # Example a=5,c=6: missing could be 4 or 7. Both give {4,5,6} or {5,6,7} which are consecutive.
                # However, condition also requires that the set equals {n,n+1,n+2} for some n. Both are valid.
                # So there are two possibilities! Wait earlier we thought unique. Let's reconsider.
                # Indeed, if a and c differ by 1, there are two possible triples: {a-1,a,c} or {a,c,c+1}. Both contain a and c and are consecutive.
                # So b could be either min-1 or max+1.
                # So there are two possibilities.
                # We'll need to branch.
                pass

[CODE] (turn 2):
def reachable_heights(N):
    # DP set of possible heights after i steps staying nonnegative
    cur = {0}
    for _ in range(N):
        nxt = set()
        for h in cur:
            for step in (-2,-1,1,2):
                nh = h + step
                if nh >= 0:
                    nxt.add(nh)
        cur = nxt
    return cur

def smallest_k(N,k):
    reachable = reachable_heights(N)
    sorted_vals = sorted(reachable)
    return sorted_vals[:k], len(sorted_vals)

for N in range(1,11):
    vals,_ = smallest_k(N,20)
    print(N, vals[:10])

[CODE] (turn 6):
def construct_grid(X, Y, h_bottom, v_right):
    # h_bottom length X (for i=0..X-1)
    # v_right length Y (for j=0..Y-1)
    # Determine T(k) for each diagonal based on bottom or right values.
    # We'll assign T(k) arbitrarily consistent with known h or v.
    # Then assign (v,h) for each node using positive pattern if possible.
    max_k = X+Y
    T = {}
    # First assign T for diagonals that intersect bottom row or right column
    # bottom row nodes (i,0) have diagonal k=i
    for i, h in enumerate(h_bottom):
        k = i
        # Determine possible T values that allow this h
        possible_T = []
        # Check each T value
        for t in (-2,-1,1,2):
            # allowed h values for this T
            allowed_h = []
            if t == 2:
                allowed_h = [-1]
            elif t == -2:
                allowed_h = [1]
            elif t == 1:
                allowed_h = [1, -2]
            elif t == -1:
                allowed_h = [2, -1]
            if h in allowed_h:
                possible_T.append(t)
        if not possible_T:
            raise ValueError("No T for h")
        # Choose first possible T (could be any)
        T[k] = possible_T[0]
    # right column nodes (X, j) have diagonal k = X + j
    for j, v in enumerate(v_right):
        k = X + j
        possible_T = []
        for t in (-2,-1,1,2):
            allowed_v = []
            if t == 2:
                allowed_v = [1]
            elif t == -2:
                allowed_v = [-1]
            elif t == 1:
                allowed_v = [2, -1]
            elif t == -1:
                allowed_v = [1, -2]
            if v in allowed_v:
                possible_T.append(t)
        if not possible_T:
            raise ValueError("No T for v")
        T[k] = possible_T[0]
    # For other diagonals, assign arbitrary T, say 2
    for k in range(max_k+1):
        if k not in T:
            T[k] = 2  # arbitrary
    # Now assign (v,h) for each node using positive pattern if possible
    v = [[None]*(Y+1) for _ in range(X+1)]
    h = [[None]*(Y+1) for _ in range(X+1)]
    for x in range(X+1):
        for y in range(Y+1):
            k = x+y
            t = T[k]
            # Determine possible pairs
            pairs = []
            if t == 2:
                pairs = [(1,-1)]
            elif t == -2:
                pairs = [(-1,1)]
            elif t == 1:
                pairs = [(2,1), (-1,-2)]
            elif t == -1:
                pairs = [(1,2), (-2,-1)]
            # Choose pair that matches known h or v if already set
            chosen = None
            # If this node is on bottom row (y=0) we already have h
            if y == 0 and x < X:
                # h must match h_bottom[x]
                for p in pairs:
                    if p[1] == h_bottom[x]:
                        chosen = p
                        break
            # If node is on right column (x==X) and y<Y, match v
            if chosen is None and x == X and y < Y:
                for p in pairs:
                    if p[0] == v_right[y]:
                        chosen = p
                        break
            # If still none, choose the pair with larger values (positive pattern)
            if chosen is None:
                # pick pair with max sum maybe
                chosen = max(pairs, key=lambda p: p[0]+p[1])
            v[x][y], h[x][y] = chosen
    # Now compute g
    g = [[None]*(Y+1) for _ in range(X+1)]
    g[0][0] = 0
    for x in range(X+1):
        for y in range(Y+1):
            if x==0 and y==0: continue
            # compute from left if possible
            if x>0:
                left = g[x-1][y] + h[x-1][y]
                if left < 0:
                    return None
                if g[x][y] is None:
                    g[x][y] = left
                elif g[x][y] != left:
                    return None
            # compute from below if possible
            if y>0:
                below = g[x][y-1] + v[x][y-1]
                if below < 0:
                    return None
                if g[x][y] is None:
                    g[x][y] = below
                elif g[x][y] != below:
                    return None
    return g

def test_random(X,Y):
    import random
    # generate random walk steps for bottom and right that stay nonnegative
    # produce h_bottom sequence length X
    # we need to ensure cumulative sum nonnegative
    h_seq = []
    cur = 0
    for i in range(X):
        step = random.choice([-2,-1,1,2])
        # ensure cur+step >=0
        if cur+step < 0:
            # choose a positive step
            step = random.choice([1,2])
        h_seq.append(step)
        cur += step
    # similarly v_seq length Y
    v_seq = []
    cur = 0
    for i in range(Y):
        step = random.choice([-2,-1,1,2])
        if cur+step < 0:
            step = random.choice([1,2])
        v_seq.append(step)
        cur += step
    g = construct_grid(X,Y,h_seq,v_seq)
    return g is not None

# test many random
for X,Y in [(3,3),(4,4),(5,5)]:
    ok = all(test_random(X,Y) for _ in range(100))
    print(X,Y, ok)

    # v_right length Y (for j=0..Y-1)

import random, itertools, math, sys, collections, functools, itertools, numpy as np, sympy as sp
def compute_residues(max_x, max_y, row0, col0):
    # row0: list of residues for (0,y) for y=0..max_y
    # col0: list of residues for (x,0) for x=0..max_x
    r = [[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    for y in range(max_y+1):
        r[0][y]=row0[y]
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        r[x][0]=col0[x]
    # ensure (0,0) consistent
    assert r[0][0]==row0[0]==col0[0]
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            # compute r[x+1][y] using r[x][y] and r[x][y+1]
            r[x+1][y]=(- (r[x][y]+r[x][y+1]))%3
    # need also compute remaining cells where y>0 and x>0 but not covered? The loop above fills all (x+1,y) for x from 0..max_x-1, y from 0..max_y-1.
    # This fills all cells except maybe last column y=max_y? Actually for y = max_y, we didn't compute r[x+1][max_y] because need r[x][max_y+1] which not defined.
    # So we need also recurrence using column? Another recurrence: r[x][y+1] = - (r[x][y]+r[x+1][y]) mod 3.
    # We'll fill iteratively scanning rows then columns.
    for y in range(max_y):
        for x in range(max_x):
            # compute r[x][y+1] using known r[x][y] and r[x+1][y]
            if r[x][y+1] is None:
                r[x][y+1]=(- (r[x][y]+r[x+1][y]))%3
    # Might still have None for (max_x, max_y) etc. We'll repeat until all filled.
    changed=True
    while changed:
        changed=False
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            for y in range(max_y+1):
                if r[x][y] is None:
                    # try compute from left or below if possible
                    if x>0 and r[x-1][y] is not None and r[x-1][y+1] is not None if y+1<=max_y else False:
                        pass
    return r

import random, itertools, math, sys, collections, functools, itertools, numpy as np, sympy as sp
def compute_residues(max_x, max_y, row0, col0):
    # row0: list of residues for (0,y) for y=0..max_y
    # col0: list of residues for (x,0) for x=0..max_x
    r = [[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    for y in range(max_y+1):
        r[0][y]=row0[y]
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        r[x][0]=col0[x]
    # ensure (0,0) consistent
    assert r[0][0]==row0[0]==col0[0]
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            # compute r[x+1][y] using r[x][y] and r[x][y+1]
            r[x+1][y]=(- (r[x][y]+r[x][y+1]))%3
    # need also compute remaining cells where y>0 and x>0 but not covered? The loop above fills all (x+1,y) for x from 0..max_x-1, y from 0..max_y-1.
    # This fills all cells except maybe last column y=max_y? Actually for y = max_y, we didn't compute r[x+1][max_y] because need r[x][max_y+1] which not defined.
    # So we need also recurrence using column? Another recurrence: r[x][y+1] = - (r[x][y]+r[x+1][y]) mod 3.
    # We'll fill iteratively scanning rows then columns.
    for y in range(max_y):
        for x in range(max_x):
            # compute r[x][y+1] using known r[x][y] and r[x+1][y]
            if r[x][y+1] is None:
                r[x][y+1]=(- (r[x][y]+r[x+1][y]))%3
    # Might still have None for (max_x, max_y) etc. We'll repeat until all filled.
    changed=True
    while changed:
        changed=False
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            for y in range(max_y+1):
                if r[x][y] is None:
                    # try compute from left or below if possible
                    if x>0 and r[x-1][y] is not None and r[x-1][y+1] is not None if y+1<=max_y else False:
                        pass
    return r

import itertools
def solve_residues(max_x, max_y, row0, col0):
    # unknowns for all cells (i,j)
    # map to index
    idx = {}
    var_list = []
    for i in range(max_x+1):
        for j in range(max_y+1):
            idx[(i,j)] = len(var_list)
            var_list.append((i,j))
    n = len(var_list)
    # equations list of (coeffs dict, rhs)
    equations = []
    # boundary constraints: r = given
    for j in range(max_y+1):
        equations.append( ({idx[(0,j)]:1}, row0[j]%3) )
    for i in range(max_x+1):
        equations.append( ({idx[(i,0)]:1}, col0[i]%3) )
    # interior equations for each (i,j) with i<max_x, j<max_y
    for i in range(max_x):
        for j in range(max_y):
            coeff = {idx[(i,j)]:1, idx[(i,j+1)]:1, idx[(i+1,j)]:1}
            equations.append( (coeff, 0) )
    # Solve linear system over Z3 using Gaussian elimination
    # matrix A (m x n) and b
    m = len(equations)
    A = [[0]*n for _ in range(m)]
    b = [0]*m
    for eq_i, (coeff, rhs) in enumerate(equations):
        for var, c in coeff.items():
            A[eq_i][var] = c % 3
        b[eq_i] = rhs % 3
    # Gaussian elimination
    A = [list(row) for row in A]
    row = 0
    col = 0
    where = [-1]*n
    while row < m and col < n:
        # find pivot
        sel = None
        for i in range(row, m):
            if A[i][col] % 3 != 0:
                sel = i
                break
        if sel is None:
            col += 1
            continue
        # swap rows
        A[row], A[sel] = A[sel], A[row]
        b[row], b[sel] = b[sel], b[row]
        # normalize pivot to 1
        inv = {1:1, 2:2}  # since 2*2=4≡1 mod3, inverse of 2 is 2
        factor = A[row][col] % 3
        if factor != 1:
            mul = inv[factor]
            for j in range(col, n):
                A[row][j] = (A[row][j]*mul) % 3
            b[row] = (b[row]*mul) % 3
        # eliminate other rows
        for i in range(m):
            if i != row and A[i][col] % 3 != 0:
                factor = A[i][col] % 3
                # subtract factor * pivot row
                for j in range(col, n):
                    A[i][j] = (A[i][j] - factor*A[row][j]) % 3
                b[i] = (b[i] - factor*b[row]) % 3
        where[col] = row
        row += 1
        col += 1
    # check consistency
    for i in range(row, m):
        if all(v%3==0 for v in A[i]) and b[i]%3 != 0:
            raise ValueError("Inconsistent")
    # free variables
    solution = [0]*n
    for i in range(n):
        if where[i] != -1:
            solution[i] = b[where[i]] % 3
        else:
            solution[i] = None  # free
    return solution, where, idx

import itertools
def solve_residues(max_x, max_y, row0, col0):
    # unknowns for all cells (i,j)
    # map to index
    idx = {}
    var_list = []
    for i in range(max_x+1):
        for j in range(max_y+1):
            idx[(i,j)] = len(var_list)
            var_list.append((i,j))
    n = len(var_list)
    # equations list of (coeffs dict, rhs)
    equations = []
    # boundary constraints: r = given
    for j in range(max_y+1):
        equations.append( ({idx[(0,j)]:1}, row0[j]%3) )
    for i in range(max_x+1):
        equations.append( ({idx[(i,0)]:1}, col0[i]%3) )
    # interior equations for each (i,j) with i<max_x, j<max_y
    for i in range(max_x):
        for j in range(max_y):
            coeff = {idx[(i,j)]:1, idx[(i,j+1)]:1, idx[(i+1,j)]:1}
            equations.append( (coeff, 0) )
    # Solve linear system over Z3 using Gaussian elimination
    # matrix A (m x n) and b
    m = len(equations)
    A = [[0]*n for _ in range(m)]
    b = [0]*m
    for eq_i, (coeff, rhs) in enumerate(equations):
        for var, c in coeff.items():
            A[eq_i][var] = c % 3
        b[eq_i] = rhs % 3
    # Gaussian elimination
    A = [list(row) for row in A]
    row = 0
    col = 0
    where = [-1]*n
    while row < m and col < n:
        # find pivot
        sel = None
        for i in range(row, m):
            if A[i][col] % 3 != 0:
                sel = i
                break
        if sel is None:
            col += 1
            continue
        # swap rows
        A[row], A[sel] = A[sel], A[row]
        b[row], b[sel] = b[sel], b[row]
        # normalize pivot to 1
        inv = {1:1, 2:2}  # since 2*2=4≡1 mod3, inverse of 2 is 2
        factor = A[row][col] % 3
        if factor != 1:
            mul = inv[factor]
            for j in range(col, n):
                A[row][j] = (A[row][j]*mul) % 3
            b[row] = (b[row]*mul) % 3
        # eliminate other rows
        for i in range(m):
            if i != row and A[i][col] % 3 != 0:
                factor = A[i][col] % 3
                # subtract factor * pivot row
                for j in range(col, n):
                    A[i][j] = (A[i][j] - factor*A[row][j]) % 3
                b[i] = (b[i] - factor*b[row]) % 3
        where[col] = row
        row += 1
        col += 1
    # check consistency
    for i in range(row, m):
        if all(v%3==0 for v in A[i]) and b[i]%3 != 0:
            raise ValueError("Inconsistent")
    # free variables
    solution = [0]*n
    for i in range(n):
        if where[i] != -1:
            solution[i] = b[where[i]] % 3
        else:
            solution[i] = None  # free
    return solution, where, idx

[CODE] (turn 3):
def random_boundary(max_x, max_y):
    row0 = [random.randint(0,2) for _ in range(max_y+1)]
    col0 = [random.randint(0,2) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    row0[0]=col0[0]=0
    return row0, col0

def get_residue_grid(max_x, max_y, row0, col0):
    sol, where, idx = solve_residues(max_x, max_y, row0, col0)
    n = (max_x+1)*(max_y+1)
    # fill free variables arbitrarily (choose 0)
    for i in range(n):
        if sol[i] is None:
            sol[i]=0
    # construct grid
    grid = [[0]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    for (i,j), var in idx.items():
        grid[i][j]=sol[var]
    return grid

row0, col0 = random_boundary(3,3)
grid = get_residue_grid(3,3, row0, col0)
print("row0", row0)
print("col0", col0)
print(grid)

[CODE] (turn 4):
import itertools

def check_grid(grid):
    # grid is dict (x,y) -> value
    # assume size up to max_x,max_y
    max_x = max(x for x,_ in grid)
    max_y = max(y for _,y in grid)
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            vals = [grid[(x,y)], grid[(x,y+1)], grid[(x+1,y)]]
            if sorted(vals) != [min(vals), min(vals)+1, min(vals)+2]:
                return False
    return True

def brute(max_x, max_y):
    # values limited 0..2 (since we think)
    all_vals = list(range(3))
    solutions = []
    for assign in itertools.product(all_vals, repeat=(max_x+1)*(max_y+1)):
        # map to dict
        grid={}
        idx=0
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            for y in range(max_y+1):
                grid[(x,y)]=assign[idx]
                idx+=1
        if grid[(0,0)]!=0: continue
        if check_grid(grid):
            solutions.append(grid)
    return solutions

sols = brute(2,2)
len(sols)

    # assume size up to max_x,max_y
    max_x = max(x for x,_ in grid)
    max_y = max(y for _,y in grid)
    for x in range(max_x):

            if sorted(vals) != [min(vals), min(vals)+1, min(vals)+2]:
                return False
    return True

def check_residue_grid(grid, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            s = (grid[(x,y)] + grid[(x,y+1)] + grid[(x+1,y)]) % 3

    return True

def check_linear(a,b,c, max_x=5, max_y=5):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            vals = [a*x+b*y+c,
                    a*x+b*(y+1)+c,
                    a*(x+1)+b*y+c]
            # need to be three consecutive integers in some order
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
    return True

solutions=[]
for a in range(-2,3):
    for b in range(-2,3):
        for c in range(0,3):
            if check_linear(a,b,c,4,4):
                solutions.append((a,b,c))
solutions

def brute_limited(max_x, max_y, max_val):
    all_vals = list(range(max_val+1))
    solutions = []
    for assign in itertools.product(all_vals, repeat=(max_x+1)*(max_y+1)):
        grid={}
        idx=0
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            for y in range(max_y+1):
                grid[(x,y)]=assign[idx]
                idx+=1
        if grid[(0,0)]!=0: continue
        if check_grid(grid):
            solutions.append(grid)
    return solutions

from functools import lru_cache

def reachable_XY(X,Y):
    # row0 values: start at 0, each step +1 or +2 (cannot go negative)
    # We'll generate all possible row0 sequences up to X (inclusive)
    reachable = set()
    # generate all possible row0 arrays length X+1
    def gen_row0(pos, seq):
        if pos == X+1:
            # compute full grid
            # initialize grid dict
            g = {}
            for x in range(X+1):
                g[(x,0)] = seq[x]
            # compute rows 1..Y
            for y in range(1,Y+1):
                for x in range(X):
                    a = g[(x,y-1)]
                    b = g[(x+1,y-1)]
                    # determine missing third number to make consecutive
                    # they differ by 1 or 2
                    if abs(a-b)==1:
                        # missing is the third number outside the interval
                        c = min(a,b)-1 if min(a,b)>0 else max(a,b)+1
                        # but ensure non-negative
                        if c<0:
                            return
                    elif abs(a-b)==2:
                        c = (a+b)//2
                    else:
                        return
                    g[(x,y)] = c
                # need also last column x=X? For x=X, triple involves (X,y-1), (X+1?, not exist) so condition not needed? Actually condition for (X,y-1) involves (X,y-1), (X,y), (X+1,y-1) which is out of grid. So we don't need value at (X,y) for our target if X,Y within grid? But we need g(X,Y) which is defined when x<X? Actually for x=X, we need g(X,Y) but we need to compute it using triple with (X,Y-1) and (X+1,Y-1) which is out of grid. So our DP limited to interior points where x<X. So we need to consider grid larger by 1 in x dimension to compute value at X,Y. Let's extend grid to X+1 columns.
                pass

[CODE] (turn 12):
from functools import lru_cache

def reachable_XY(X,Y):
    # row0 values: start at 0, each step +1 or +2 (cannot go negative)
    # We'll generate all possible row0 sequences up to X (inclusive)
    reachable = set()
    # generate all possible row0 arrays length X+1
    def gen_row0(pos, seq):
        if pos == X+1:
            # compute full grid
            # initialize grid dict
            g = {}
            for x in range(X+1):
                g[(x,0)] = seq[x]
            # compute rows 1..Y
            for y in range(1,Y+1):
                for x in range(X):
                    a = g[(x,y-1)]
                    b = g[(x+1,y-1)]
                    # determine missing third number to make consecutive
                    # they differ by 1 or 2
                    if abs(a-b)==1:
                        # missing is the third number outside the interval
                        c = min(a,b)-1 if min(a,b)>0 else max(a,b)+1
                        # but ensure non-negative
                        if c<0:
                            return
                    elif abs(a-b)==2:
                        c = (a+b)//2
                    else:
                        return
                    g[(x,y)] = c
                # need also last column x=X? For x=X, triple involves (X,y-1), (X+1?, not exist) so condition not needed? Actually condition for (X,y-1) involves (X,y-1), (X,y), (X+1,y-1) which is out of grid. So we don't need value at (X,y) for our target if X,Y within grid? But we need g(X,Y) which is defined when x<X? Actually for x=X, we need g(X,Y) but we need to compute it using triple with (X,Y-1) and (X+1,Y-1) which is out of grid. So our DP limited to interior points where x<X. So we need to consider grid larger by 1 in x dimension to compute value at X,Y. Let's extend grid to X+1 columns.
                pass

[CODE] (turn 13):
def reachable_XY(X,Y):
    # generate row0 values for x=0..X+1
    max_len = X+2
    reachable = set()
    def gen_row0(pos, seq):
        if pos == max_len:
            # compute grid up to X+1 columns, Y rows
            g = {}
            for x in range(max_len):
                g[(x,0)] = seq[x]
            # compute rows 1..Y
            for y in range(1,Y+1):
                for x in range(max_len-1):
                    a = g[(x,y-1)]
                    b = g[(x+1,y-1)]
                    # determine missing third number
                    if abs(a-b)==1:
                        # missing is the third number outside interval
                        # interval is [min, max]; third is min-1 if >=0 else max+1
                        mn = min(a,b)
                        mx = max(a,b)
                        if mn>0:
                            c = mn-1
                        else:
                            c = mx+1
                    elif abs(a-b)==2:
                        c = (a+b)//2
                    else:
                        return
                    g[(x,y)] = c
            reachable.add(g[(X,Y)])
            return
        # generate next value: can be previous +/-1 or +/-2 as long as >=0
        prev = seq[-1] if seq else None
        if prev is None:
            # first value must be 0
            gen_row0(pos+1, seq+[0])
        else:
            for delta in (1,2,-1,-2):
                val = prev + delta
                if val >= 0:
                    gen_row0(pos+1, seq+[val])
    gen_row0(0, [])
    return reachable

reach = reachable_XY(2,2)

[CODE] (turn 14):
def reachable_XY_dp(X,Y, max_val):
    # DP over rows: keep possible values for column X (current row) and column X+1 (current row)
    # row0 values for columns 0..X+1 can be any sequence with adjacent diff 1 or 2, values >=0, and <=max_val
    # We'll generate all possible row0 sequences within bound.
    from itertools import product
    # generate all possible row0 sequences length X+2 with values 0..max_val and adjacent diff 1 or 2
    def gen_seq(length, start):
        seqs = [[start]]
        for _ in range(1,length):
            new = []
            for s in seqs:
                prev = s[-1]
                for delta in (1,2,-1,-2):
                    val = prev + delta
                    if 0 <= val <= max_val:
                        new.append(s+[val])
            seqs = new
        return seqs
    row0_seqs = gen_seq(X+2,0)
    reachable = set()
    for row0 in row0_seqs:
        # we also need column X+1 values for each subsequent row; we can treat them as free sequences as well.
        # We'll generate column X+1 values for rows 0..Y (length Y+1) with adjacency diff 1 or 2.
        col_seq = gen_seq(Y+1, row0[-1])  # start with row0 column X+1 value as first element (row0 at y=0)
        for col_vals in col_seq:
            # compute grid row by row
            # store values for current row (y) for columns 0..X (we will compute)
            # we have previous row values for columns 0..X+1: prev_row = row0 for y=0, else computed.
            prev = row0[:]  # length X+2
            # iterate rows 1..Y
            for y in range(1,Y+1):
                cur = [None]*(X+2)
                # compute columns 0..X using prev values and b = prev[x+1]
                for x in range(X+1):
                    a = prev[x]
                    b = prev[x+1]
                    if abs(a-b)==1:
                        mn = min(a,b)
                        mx = max(a,b)
                        if mn>0:
                            c = mn-1
                        else:
                            c = mx+1
                    elif abs(a-b)==2:
                        c = (a+b)//2
                    else:
                        break
                    cur[x]=c
                else:
                    # after loop, set column X+1 value for this row from col_vals
                    cur[X+1]=col_vals[y]
                    prev=cur
                    continue
                break
            else:
                # after rows computed, record target
                reachable.add(prev[X])  # value at (X,Y)
    return reachable

reach = reachable_XY_dp(2,2,5)
sorted(reach)

def reachable_XY_dp2(X,Y, max_val):
    # generate row0 sequences as before
    def gen_seq(length, start):
        seqs = [[start]]
        for _ in range(1,length):
            new = []
            for s in seqs:
                prev = s[-1]
                for delta in (1,2,-1,-2):
                    val = prev + delta
                    if 0 <= val <= max_val:
                        new.append(s+[val])
            seqs = new
        return seqs
    row0_seqs = gen_seq(X+2,0)
    reachable=set()
    for row0 in row0_seqs:
        # column X+1 values for rows 1..Y can be any 0..max_val
        from itertools import product
        for col_vals in product(range(max_val+1), repeat=Y+1):
            # col_vals[0] must equal row0[-1] (value at (X+1,0))
            if col_vals[0]!=row0[-1]:
                continue
            prev=row0[:]  # length X+2
            # set column X+1 for row0 already in prev
            for y in range(1,Y+1):
                cur=[None]*(X+2)
                for x in range(X+1):
                    a=prev[x]
                    b=prev[x+1]
                    if abs(a-b)==1:
                        mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                        if mn>0:
                            c=mn-1
                        else:
                            c=mx+1
                    elif abs(a-b)==2:
                        c=(a+b)//2
                    else:
                        break
                    cur[x]=c
                else:
                    cur[X+1]=col_vals[y]
                    prev=cur
                    continue
                break
            else:
                reachable.add(prev[X])
    return reachable

from functools import lru_cache

def enumerate_grid(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size (max_x+1) by (max_y+1)
    # returns set of possible values at (max_x, max_y)
    # using backtracking with pruning
    # Represent grid as dict of (x,y)->value
    values = {}
    results=set()
    # order of assignment: row-major
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # but constraints involve triples (x,y),(x+1,y),(x,y+1) where x<max_x, y<max_y
    # We'll check constraint when all three assigned.
    # Use recursion
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            # all assigned, record value at target
            results.add(values[(max_x,max_y)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values for this cell based on constraints with already assigned neighbors.
        # Since values must be natural numbers (>=0)
        # We'll try a reasonable bound: maybe up to 2*(max_x+max_y)+5
        # But we can also compute min possible based on known neighbors.
        # For simplicity, try range 0..2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        for v in range(0, 2*(max_x+max_y)+5):
            # assign
            values[(x,y)]=v
            # check constraints that become fully assigned now
            ok=True
            # L shape with (x-1,y) as left neighbor (if x>0)
            if x>0:
                # triple (x-1,y), (x-1,y+1), (x,y)
                if y<max_y:
                    pts=[(x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)]
                    if all(p in values for p in pts):
                        vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                        if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                            ok=False
                # also triple (x-1,y-1), (x-1,y), (x,y-1)?? Not needed
            # L shape with (x,y) as top-left corner (if x<max_x and y<max_y)
            if ok and x<max_x and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape with (x,y-1) as left neighbor (if y>0)
            if ok and y>0:
                # triple (x,y-1), (x,y), (x+1,y-1) if x<max_x
                if x<max_x:
                    pts=[(x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)]
                    if all(p in values for p in pts):
                        vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                        if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                            ok=False
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            # cleanup
            del values[(x,y)]
    # set initial condition g(0,0)=0
    values[(0,0)]=0
    # start backtrack from idx=1 (skip (0,0))
    start_idx=1
    backtrack(start_idx)
    return results

print(enumerate_grid(1,1))

[CODE] (turn 2):
from functools import lru_cache

def enumerate_grid(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size (max_x+1) by (max_y+1)
    # returns set of possible values at (max_x, max_y)
    # using backtracking with pruning
    # Represent grid as dict of (x,y)->value
    values = {}
    results=set()
    # order of assignment: row-major
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # but constraints involve triples (x,y),(x+1,y),(x,y+1) where x<max_x, y<max_y
    # We'll check constraint when all three assigned.
    # Use recursion
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            # all assigned, record value at target
            results.add(values[(max_x,max_y)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values for this cell based on constraints with already assigned neighbors.
        # Since values must be natural numbers (>=0)
        # We'll try a reasonable bound: maybe up to 2*(max_x+max_y)+5
        # But we can also compute min possible based on known neighbors.
        # For simplicity, try range 0..2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        for v in range(0, 2*(max_x+max_y)+5):
            # assign
            values[(x,y)]=v
            # check constraints that become fully assigned now
            ok=True
            # L shape with (x-1,y) as left neighbor (if x>0)
            if x>0:
                # triple (x-1,y), (x-1,y+1), (x,y)
                if y<max_y:
                    pts=[(x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)]
                    if all(p in values for p in pts):
                        vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                        if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                            ok=False
                # also triple (x-1,y-1), (x-1,y), (x,y-1)?? Not needed
            # L shape with (x,y) as top-left corner (if x<max_x and y<max_y)
            if ok and x<max_x and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape with (x,y-1) as left neighbor (if y>0)
            if ok and y>0:
                # triple (x,y-1), (x,y), (x+1,y-1) if x<max_x
                if x<max_x:
                    pts=[(x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)]
                    if all(p in values for p in pts):
                        vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                        if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                            ok=False
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            # cleanup
            del values[(x,y)]
    # set initial condition g(0,0)=0
    values[(0,0)]=0
    # start backtrack from idx=1 (skip (0,0))
    start_idx=1
    backtrack(start_idx)
    return results

print(enumerate_grid(1,1))

def find_example(max_x, max_y, target):
    values={}
    cells=[(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # assign (0,0)=0
    values[(0,0)]=0
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            if values[(max_x,max_y)]==target:
                return dict(values)
            return None
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            return backtrack(idx+1)
        for v in range(0, 2*(max_x+max_y)+5):
            values[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check constraints where all three assigned
            # L shape (x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)
            if x>0 and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)
            if ok and x<max_x and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape (x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)
            if ok and y>0 and x<max_x:
                pts=[(x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            if ok:
                res=backtrack(idx+1)
                if res is not None:
                    return res
            del values[(x,y)]
        return None
    return backtrack(1)

example=find_example(1,1,8)

def exists_solution(max_x, max_y, target):
    values={}
    cells=[(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    values[(0,0)]=0
    # We'll backtrack but prune heavily; may be large.
    # Use recursion with ordering.
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            return values[(max_x,max_y)]==target
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            return backtrack(idx+1)
        # Determine possible range for value: 0..some bound
        for v in range(0, 30):  # small bound
            values[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check any L shape where all three points assigned
            # L shape with base (x,y)
            if x<max_x and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape with base (x-1,y) if x>0
            if ok and x>0 and y<max_y:
                pts=[(x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            # L shape with base (x,y-1) if y>0
            if ok and y>0 and x<max_x:
                pts=[(x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)]
                if all(p in values for p in pts):
                    vals=[values[p] for p in pts]
                    if len(set(vals))!=3 or max(vals)-min(vals)!=2:
                        ok=False
            if ok:
                if backtrack(idx+1):
                    return True
            del values[(x,y)]
        return False
    return backtrack(1)

print(exists_solution(2,2,8))

def exists_solution(max_x, max_y, target):

import random, itertools

def generate_grid(max_x, max_y, D):
    # D = -1 or 1
    # allowed pairs for D
    if D==-1:
        pairs=[(1,2),(-2,-1)]
    elif D==1:
        pairs=[(2,1),(-1,-2)]
    else:
        raise ValueError
    g=[[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    g[0][0]=0
    # We'll assign values row by row using chosen pairs.
    # For each point (x,y), we need to know a(x,y) and b(x,y) to compute neighbors.
    # We'll store a,b arrays.
    a=[[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    b=[[None]*(max_y+1) for _ in range(max_x+1)]
    # We'll fill using BFS: start from (0,0), assign pair randomly, propagate.
    # Simple approach: assign pair at each point arbitrarily, then compute g via recursion.
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        for y in range(max_y+1):
            if x==0 and y==0:
                continue
            # choose pair randomly
            a_xy,b_xy=random.choice(pairs)
            a[x][y]=a_xy
            b[x][y]=b_xy
    # Now compute g using dynamic programming: g(x,y) = g(x-1,y)+a(x-1,y) if x>0 else g(x,y-1)+b(x,y-1)
    # Need consistency: both ways must give same result; we must enforce that.
    # We'll compute g using both formulas and check consistency.
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        for y in range(max_y+1):
            if x==0 and y==0:
                continue
            vals=[]
            if x>0:
                if a[x-1][y] is None:
                    continue
                vals.append(g[x-1][y]+a[x-1][y])
            if y>0:
                if b[x][y-1] is None:
                    continue
                vals.append(g[x][y-1]+b[x][y-1])
            # they must be equal
            if len(vals)==2 and vals[0]!=vals[1]:
                return None
            g[x][y]=vals[0] if vals else None
    # verify condition
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            triple=[g[x][y], g[x][y+1], g[x+1][y]]
            if None in triple:
                continue
            if len(set(triple))!=3 or max(triple)-min(triple)!=2:
                return False
    return True

def test_random(max_x,max_y, trials=100):
    for D in [-1,1]:
        for _ in range(trials):
            ok=generate_grid(max_x,max_y,D)
            if ok is False:
                return False
    return True

print(test_random(5,5,10))

[CODE] (turn 16):
import random

def random_g(i,j, trials=10000):
    vals=set()
    for _ in range(trials):
        # D = -1
        # choose a_x for x=0..i-1 randomly from {1,-2}
        a_seq=[random.choice([1,-2]) for _ in range(i)]
        # b_y for y=0..j-1 randomly from {2,-1}
        b_seq=[random.choice([2,-1]) for _ in range(j)]
        # compute g = sum a_seq + sum b_seq
        g_val=sum(a_seq)+sum(b_seq)
        if g_val>=0:
            vals.add(g_val)
    return vals

i=5; j=7
vals=random_g(i,j,20000)
print(sorted(list(vals))[:20])
print(len(vals))

import random

def random_g(i,j, trials=10000):
    vals=set()
    for _ in range(trials):
        # D = -1
        # choose a_x for x=0..i-1 randomly from {1,-2}
        a_seq=[random.choice([1,-2]) for _ in range(i)]
        # b_y for y=0..j-1 randomly from {2,-1}
        b_seq=[random.choice([2,-1]) for _ in range(j)]
        # compute g = sum a_seq + sum b_seq
        g_val=sum(a_seq)+sum(b_seq)
        if g_val>=0:
            vals.add(g_val)
    return vals

[CODE] (turn 1):
def check_g(p,q,limit=5):
    # g(x,y)=p*x+q*y
    for x in range(limit):
        for y in range(limit):
            a = p*x+q*y
            b = p*x+q*(y+1)
            c = p*(x+1)+q*y
            s = {a,b,c}
            if len(s)!=3:
                return False
            n = min(s)
            if s != {n,n+1,n+2}:
                return False
    return True

pairs = [(1,2),(2,1),(-1,-2),(-2,-1),(1,-1),(-1,1)]
for p,q in pairs:
    print(p,q,check_g(p,q,limit=10))

[CODE] (turn 2):
import itertools

def generate_grid(m,n):
    # grid size (m+1)*(n+1) with coordinates (0..m,0..n)
    # g(0,0)=0
    # constraints for all x in 0..m-1, y in 0..n-1: triple (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y) consecutive
    # We'll brute force values within some range, say -5..5.
    vals_range = range(-5,6)
    # We'll assign values using recursion/backtracking.
    grid = {}
    grid[(0,0)] = 0
    # order of coordinates to assign: maybe row-major.
    coords = [(i,j) for i in range(m+1) for j in range(n+1) if (i,j)!=(0,0)]
    solutions = []
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(coords):
            solutions.append(dict(grid))
            return
        i,j = coords[idx]
        # possible values limited by neighbor constraints that are already assigned
        # compute allowed values based on any triple that becomes fully assigned when we assign this cell.
        # For each triple that includes (i,j) and other two already assigned, enforce condition.
        # Also for any neighbor already assigned, we need diff 1 or 2.
        # We'll generate candidate values from -10..10 maybe.
        # Use set of possible values.
        possible = set(vals_range)
        # enforce diff constraints with assigned neighbors
        for di,dj in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj = i+di, j+dj
            if (ni,nj) in grid:
                diff = grid[(ni,nj)]
                # need |v - diff| in {1,2}
                # So v must be diff +/-1 or +/-2
                allowed = {diff+1,diff-1,diff+2,diff-2}
                possible &= allowed
        # enforce triple constraints where this cell is the third cell needed
        # triples: (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y) where (i,j) could be any of them.
        # We'll check any triple fully assigned after we assign.
        # For each triple where the other two are assigned, we restrict v such that set forms consecutive numbers.
        triples = []
        # Cases where (i,j) is (x,y)
        triples.append(((i,j),(i,j+1),(i+1,j)))
        # (i,j) is (x,y+1)
        triples.append(((i,j-1),(i,j),(i+1,j-1)))
        # (i,j) is (x+1,y)
        triples.append(((i-1,j),(i-1,j+1),(i,j)))
        for t in triples:
            # check if other two are assigned
            assigned = [grid.get(coord) for coord in t]
            # count None
            if assigned.count(None)==1:
                # find index of None (the cell we are assigning)
                idx_none = assigned.index(None)
                # other two values:
                other_vals = [assigned[k] for k in range(3) if k!=idx_none]
                # Determine allowed v such that set {v, other_vals[0], other_vals[1]} are three consecutive ints.
                # Compute min and max of other_vals; they differ by either 1 or 2 (since they must be distinct and diff <=2)
                # We'll brute force candidate v from possible set.
                allowed_v = set()
                # iterate over possible values from -10..10
                for v in range(-10,11):
                    s = {v, other_vals[0], other_vals[1]}
                    if len(s)==3:
                        mn = min(s); mx = max(s)
                        if mx-mn==2:
                            allowed_v.add(v)
                possible &= allowed_v
        # if possible empty, prune
        if not possible:
            return
        for v in possible:
            grid[(i,j)] = v
            backtrack(idx+1)
            del grid[(i,j)]
    backtrack(0)
    return solutions

sol = generate_grid(2,2)

[CODE] (turn 2):
import itertools

def generate_grid(m,n):
    # grid size (m+1)*(n+1) with coordinates (0..m,0..n)
    # g(0,0)=0
    # constraints for all x in 0..m-1, y in 0..n-1: triple (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y) consecutive
    # We'll brute force values within some range, say -5..5.
    vals_range = range(-5,6)
    # We'll assign values using recursion/backtracking.
    grid = {}
    grid[(0,0)] = 0
    # order of coordinates to assign: maybe row-major.
    coords = [(i,j) for i in range(m+1) for j in range(n+1) if (i,j)!=(0,0)]
    solutions = []
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(coords):
            solutions.append(dict(grid))
            return
        i,j = coords[idx]
        # possible values limited by neighbor constraints that are already assigned
        # compute allowed values based on any triple that becomes fully assigned when we assign this cell.
        # For each triple that includes (i,j) and other two already assigned, enforce condition.
        # Also for any neighbor already assigned, we need diff 1 or 2.
        # We'll generate candidate values from -10..10 maybe.
        # Use set of possible values.
        possible = set(vals_range)
        # enforce diff constraints with assigned neighbors
        for di,dj in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj = i+di, j+dj
            if (ni,nj) in grid:
                diff = grid[(ni,nj)]
                # need |v - diff| in {1,2}
                # So v must be diff +/-1 or +/-2
                allowed = {diff+1,diff-1,diff+2,diff-2}
                possible &= allowed
        # enforce triple constraints where this cell is the third cell needed
        # triples: (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y) where (i,j) could be any of them.
        # We'll check any triple fully assigned after we assign.
        # For each triple where the other two are assigned, we restrict v such that set forms consecutive numbers.
        triples = []
        # Cases where (i,j) is (x,y)
        triples.append(((i,j),(i,j+1),(i+1,j)))
        # (i,j) is (x,y+1)
        triples.append(((i,j-1),(i,j),(i+1,j-1)))
        # (i,j) is (x+1,y)
        triples.append(((i-1,j),(i-1,j+1),(i,j)))
        for t in triples:
            # check if other two are assigned
            assigned = [grid.get(coord) for coord in t]
            # count None
            if assigned.count(None)==1:
                # find index of None (the cell we are assigning)
                idx_none = assigned.index(None)
                # other two values:
                other_vals = [assigned[k] for k in range(3) if k!=idx_none]
                # Determine allowed v such that set {v, other_vals[0], other_vals[1]} are three consecutive ints.
                # Compute min and max of other_vals; they differ by either 1 or 2 (since they must be distinct and diff <=2)
                # We'll brute force candidate v from possible set.
                allowed_v = set()
                # iterate over possible values from -10..10
                for v in range(-10,11):
                    s = {v, other_vals[0], other_vals[1]}
                    if len(s)==3:
                        mn = min(s); mx = max(s)
                        if mx-mn==2:
                            allowed_v.add(v)
                possible &= allowed_v
        # if possible empty, prune
        if not possible:
            return
        for v in possible:
            grid[(i,j)] = v
            backtrack(idx+1)
            del grid[(i,j)]
    backtrack(0)
    return solutions

sol = generate_grid(2,2)
len(sol)

def diff_grid(grid):
    diffs = {}
    for (i,j), val in grid.items():
        diffs[(i,j)] = val - (i - j)
    return diffs

def generate_grid(m,n, val_range=range(-5,6)):
    grid = {(0,0):0}
    coords = [(i,j) for i in range(m+1) for j in range(n+1) if (i,j)!=(0,0)]
    solutions = []
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(coords):
            solutions.append(dict(grid))
            return
        i,j = coords[idx]
        possible = set(val_range)
        # neighbor constraints
        for di,dj in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj = i+di, j+dj
            if (ni,nj) in grid:
                diff = grid[(ni,nj)]
                allowed = {diff+1,diff-1,diff+2,diff-2}
                possible &= allowed
        # triple constraints where this cell is missing one of three
        triples = []
        triples.append(((i,j),(i,j+1),(i+1,j)))  # (x,y) case
        triples.append(((i,j-1),(i,j),(i+1,j-1)))  # (x,y+1) case
        triples.append(((i-1,j),(i-1,j+1),(i,j)))  # (x+1,y) case
        for t in triples:
            vals = [grid.get(coord) for coord in t]
            if vals.count(None)==1:
                idx_none = vals.index(None)
                other = [v for v in vals if v is not None]
                allowed_v = set()
                for v in range(-10,11):
                    s = {v, other[0], other[1]}
                    if len(s)==3 and max(s)-min(s)==2:
                        allowed_v.add(v)
                possible &= allowed_v
        if not possible:
            return
        for v in possible:
            grid[(i,j)] = v
            backtrack(idx+1)
            del grid[(i,j)]
    backtrack(0)
    return solutions

def all_values_at(m,n,target):
    sols = generate_grid(m,n)
    return {sol[target] for sol in sols if target in sol}

import itertools

S = {(1,2),(2,1),(-1,-2),(-2,-1),(1,-1),(-1,1)}
values = [1,2,-1,-2]
def valid_subset(H,V):
    for h in H:
        for v in V:
            if (h,v) not in S:
                return False
    return True

valid_pairs = []
for Hmask in range(1<<4):
    H = {values[i] for i in range(4) if Hmask>>i &1}
    for Vmask in range(1<<4):
        V = {values[i] for i in range(4) if Vmask>>i &1}
        if H and V and valid_subset(H,V):
            valid_pairs.append((frozenset(H), frozenset(V)))
len(valid_pairs)
