from functools import lru_cache

def possible_values(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size inclusive 0..max_x, 0..max_y
    # Use backtracking with recursion, assign values row by row.
    # Represent grid as dict (x,y)->value
    values = {}
    results = set()
    # Pre-assign (0,0)=0
    values[(0,0)] = 0
    
    # order of cells: maybe iterate over increasing x+y? We'll just linear order.
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    
    # Pre-calc squares that involve each cell: squares lower-left (x,y) where cell is one of three corners.
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            # square lower-left (x,y) involves (x,y), (x,y+1), (x+1,y)
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    
    # Helper to check condition for a square if its three cells are assigned.
    def check_square(x,y):
        # cells
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    
    # Backtrack
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx == len(cells):
            # all assigned, record value at (max_x, max_y)
            results.add(values[(max_x, max_y)])
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values: maybe we can bound? Not obvious.
        # We'll try a reasonable range: maybe from -5 to 5? but values can grow.
        # For small grid, we can try limited range and see possibilities.
        # Let's compute constraints from squares that become fully known after assigning this cell.
        # For each square that includes this cell, if the other two cells are already assigned, then we can deduce that the value must be one of three consecutive numbers containing those two.
        possible = set()
        # Determine constraints from each square
        constraints = []
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0 = sq
            # other two cells
            other = [(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b = values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                # The three numbers must be consecutive; we have two known numbers.
                # Determine possible third numbers that make set consecutive.
                # Let set {a,b,c} be consecutive.
                # So max-min =2 and they are distinct.
                # So c must be such that max(a,b,c)-min(a,b,c)=2 and distinct.
                # Compute possible c values.
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                # Cases:
                # If mx - mn == 2: then a,b already span 2, so c must be the middle value (mn+1) if not already present.
                # If mx - mn == 1: then they are consecutive, missing either mn-1 or mx+1.
                # If mx - mn == 0: they are equal, then c must be mn+1 or mn+2? Actually need three distinct consecutive numbers, so cannot have equal values. So this case invalid.
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    # missing middle
                    mid = mn+1
                    if mid not in (a,b):
                        constraints.append({mid})
                    else:
                        # already have three distinct? but a,b already distinct and span 2, they must be mn and mx, missing middle.
                        constraints.append({mid})
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    # missing either mn-1 or mx+1
                    cand = {mn-1, mx+1}
                    # ensure not equal to existing values
                    cand = {c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    # mx - mn >2 or =0 => impossible, no possible c
                    return
        # Intersect constraints
        if constraints:
            possible = set.intersection(*constraints) if constraints else set()
        else:
            # No constraints yet, allow a range. Let's bound values to reasonable range.
            # For small grid, we can allow -5..5 maybe.
            possible = set(range(-5, 6))
        # Also need to ensure after assigning, all squares that become fully known satisfy condition.
        # We'll try each possible value.
        for val in possible:
            values[cell] = val
            # check all squares that involve this cell (any that become fully known)
            ok = True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return results

print(possible_values(2,2))

from functools import lru_cache

def possible_values(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size inclusive 0..max_x, 0..max_y
    # Use backtracking with recursion, assign values row by row.
    # Represent grid as dict (x,y)->value
    values = {}
    results = set()
    # Pre-assign (0,0)=0
    values[(0,0)] = 0
    
    # order of cells: maybe iterate over increasing x+y? We'll just linear order.
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    
    # Pre-calc squares that involve each cell: squares lower-left (x,y) where cell is one of three corners.
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            # square lower-left (x,y) involves (x,y), (x,y+1), (x+1,y)
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    
    # Helper to check condition for a square if its three cells are assigned.
    def check_square(x,y):
        # cells
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    
    # Backtrack
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx == len(cells):
            # all assigned, record value at (max_x, max_y)
            results.add(values[(max_x, max_y)])
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values: maybe we can bound? Not obvious.
        # We'll try a reasonable range: maybe from -5 to 5? but values can grow.
        # For small grid, we can try limited range and see possibilities.
        # Let's compute constraints from squares that become fully known after assigning this cell.
        # For each square that includes this cell, if the other two cells are already assigned, then we can deduce that the value must be one of three consecutive numbers containing those two.
        possible = set()
        # Determine constraints from each square
        constraints = []
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0 = sq
            # other two cells
            other = [(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b = values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                # The three numbers must be consecutive; we have two known numbers.
                # Determine possible third numbers that make set consecutive.
                # Let set {a,b,c} be consecutive.
                # So max-min =2 and they are distinct.
                # So c must be such that max(a,b,c)-min(a,b,c)=2 and distinct.
                # Compute possible c values.
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                # Cases:
                # If mx - mn == 2: then a,b already span 2, so c must be the middle value (mn+1) if not already present.
                # If mx - mn == 1: then they are consecutive, missing either mn-1 or mx+1.
                # If mx - mn == 0: they are equal, then c must be mn+1 or mn+2? Actually need three distinct consecutive numbers, so cannot have equal values. So this case invalid.
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    # missing middle
                    mid = mn+1
                    if mid not in (a,b):
                        constraints.append({mid})
                    else:
                        # already have three distinct? but a,b already distinct and span 2, they must be mn and mx, missing middle.
                        constraints.append({mid})
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    # missing either mn-1 or mx+1
                    cand = {mn-1, mx+1}
                    # ensure not equal to existing values
                    cand = {c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    # mx - mn >2 or =0 => impossible, no possible c
                    return
        # Intersect constraints
        if constraints:
            possible = set.intersection(*constraints) if constraints else set()
        else:
            # No constraints yet, allow a range. Let's bound values to reasonable range.
            # For small grid, we can allow -5..5 maybe.
            possible = set(range(-5, 6))
        # Also need to ensure after assigning, all squares that become fully known satisfy condition.
        # We'll try each possible value.
        for val in possible:
            values[cell] = val
            # check all squares that involve this cell (any that become fully known)
            ok = True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return results

def possible_values_range(max_x, max_y, bound=10):
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    results = set()
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx == len(cells):
            results.add(values[(max_x, max_y)])
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        # Determine constraints from squares where other two assigned
        constraints = []
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0 = sq
            other = [(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b = values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    mid = mn+1
                    constraints.append({mid})
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    cand = {mn-1, mx+1}
                    cand = {c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible = set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible = set(range(-bound, bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell] = val
            ok = True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return results

def find_example(max_x,max_y,bound=20):
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    result = None
    def backtrack(idx):
        nonlocal result
        if result is not None:
            return
        if idx == len(cells):
            result = dict(values)
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        constraints = []
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0 = sq
            other = [(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b = values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    cand = {mn-1, mx+1}
                    cand = {c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible = set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible = set(range(-bound,bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return result

def max_example(max_x,max_y,bound=20):
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    best = None
    best_val = -10**9
    def backtrack(idx):
        nonlocal best, best_val
        if idx == len(cells):
            val = values[(max_x,max_y)]
            if val > best_val:
                best_val = val
                best = dict(values)
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        constraints = []
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0 = sq
            other = [(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b = values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    cand = {mn-1, mx+1}
                    cand = {c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible = set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible = set(range(-bound,bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return best_val, best

def verify(grid, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            vals = [grid[(x,y)], grid[(x,y+1)], grid[(x+1,y)]]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False, (x,y,vals)
    return True, None

val, example = max_example(2,3,bound=30)
print(val)
ok, info = verify(example,2,3)
print(ok, info)

def verify(grid, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            vals = [grid[(x,y)], grid[(x,y+1)], grid[(x+1,y)]]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False, (x,y,vals)
    return True, None

def possible_values_at_target(max_x, max_y, target, bound=30):
    # target is (tx,ty) within region
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    results=set()
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            results.add(values[target])
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible=set(range(-bound,bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return results

res = possible_values_at_target(3,4,(2,3), bound=30)
print(sorted(res)[:10], len(res))

                    constraints.append({mn+1})

                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}

                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return

            possible=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible=set(range(-bound,bound+1))

                    break

def generate_residues(max_x, max_y, axis_seq):
    # axis_seq list of residues for (x,0) for x=0..max_x
    c = {}
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        c[(x,0)] = axis_seq[x]
    # compute rows y=1..max_y
    for y in range(1, max_y+1):
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            if x==max_x:
                # need c(x+1,y-1) which is out of range; we cannot compute using square (x,y-1) because x+1 > max_x.
                # But we could compute using square (x-1,y-1) if x>0.
                # So we need to handle boundary: we cannot compute residues for rightmost column using this recurrence alone.
                # However the condition must hold for all squares, including those with lower-left (max_x-1, y-1) which involve (max_x, y-1) and (max_x, y).
                # So we can compute c(max_x, y) using square (max_x-1, y-1) where we know c(max_x-1, y-1) and c(max_x, y-1) (already known) -> compute c(max_x-1, y)?? Wait square (max_x-1, y-1) includes (max_x-1, y-1), (max_x, y-1), (max_x-1, y). So we can compute c(max_x-1, y) not c(max_x, y).
                # So we need a different approach: compute column by column using squares with lower-left (x, y-1) for x from 0..max_x-1 to compute c(x, y). This leaves the rightmost column (x=max_x) undefined.
                # But we can also compute using squares with lower-left (max_x, y-1) which involve (max_x, y-1), (max_x, y), (max_x+1, y-1) (outside). So not helpful.
                # So to compute full infinite grid, we need axis values along both axes (x=0 column and y=0 row) to determine interior uniquely.
                pass

[CODE] (turn 13):
def generate_residues(max_x, max_y, axis_seq):
    # axis_seq list of residues for (x,0) for x=0..max_x
    c = {}
    for x in range(max_x+1):
        c[(x,0)] = axis_seq[x]
    # compute rows y=1..max_y
    for y in range(1, max_y+1):
        for x in range(max_x+1):
            if x==max_x:
                # need c(x+1,y-1) which is out of range; we cannot compute using square (x,y-1) because x+1 > max_x.
                # But we could compute using square (x-1,y-1) if x>0.
                # So we need to handle boundary: we cannot compute residues for rightmost column using this recurrence alone.
                # However the condition must hold for all squares, including those with lower-left (max_x-1, y-1) which involve (max_x, y-1) and (max_x, y).
                # So we can compute c(max_x, y) using square (max_x-1, y-1) where we know c(max_x-1, y-1) and c(max_x, y-1) (already known) -> compute c(max_x-1, y)?? Wait square (max_x-1, y-1) includes (max_x-1, y-1), (max_x, y-1), (max_x-1, y). So we can compute c(max_x-1, y) not c(max_x, y).
                # So we need a different approach: compute column by column using squares with lower-left (x, y-1) for x from 0..max_x-1 to compute c(x, y). This leaves the rightmost column (x=max_x) undefined.
                # But we can also compute using squares with lower-left (max_x, y-1) which involve (max_x, y-1), (max_x, y), (max_x+1, y-1) (outside). So not helpful.
                # So to compute full infinite grid, we need axis values along both axes (x=0 column and y=0 row) to determine interior uniquely.
                pass

[CODE] (turn 14):
import random, collections, sys, math

def random_function(max_x, max_y):
    # We'll maintain values dict, start with (0,0)=0.
    values = {(0,0):0}
    # We'll maintain list of squares needing processing.
    # Each square lower-left (x,y) for 0<=x<max_x, 0<=y<max_y.
    squares = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x) for y in range(max_y)]
    # We'll iteratively assign values to cells when possible.
    # We'll keep a set of cells that are not yet assigned.
    # We'll loop until all cells assigned.
    # Use while loop with progress.
    while True:
        progress=False
        # For each square, check known count
        for (x,y) in squares:
            cells = [(x,y),(x+1,y),(x,y+1)]
            known = [c for c in cells if c in values]
            unknown = [c for c in cells if c not in values]
            if len(unknown)==1:
                # Determine value for unknown cell
                a,b = values[known[0]], values[known[1]]
                # Determine possible third values
                mn = min(a,b)
                mx = max(a,b)
                if mx - mn == 2:
                    # third must be middle
                    c_val = mn+1
                    # assign
                    values[unknown[0]] = c_val
                    progress=True
                elif mx - mn == 1:
                    # two possibilities
                    cand = [mn-1, mx+1]
                    # choose randomly among those not equal to a or b
                    cand = [c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)]
                    c_val = random.choice(cand)
                    values[unknown[0]] = c_val
                    progress=True
                else:
                    # cannot determine (difference 0 or >2) -> invalid
                    raise ValueError("Invalid known values")
        if not progress:
            # No more deterministic assignments; need to pick a square with two unknowns? We'll need to assign arbitrarily.
            # Find a cell not assigned yet, assign arbitrary value (e.g., 0) and continue.
            # Choose a cell with minimal coordinates not assigned.
            for x in range(max_x+1):
                for y in range(max_y+1):
                    if (x,y) not in values:
                        values[(x,y)] = random.randint(-10,10)
                        progress=True
                        break
                if progress:
                    break
        if len(values)==(max_x+1)*(max_y+1):
            break
    return values

def sample_values(max_x,max_y, trials=1000):
    vals=set()
    for _ in range(trials):
        v = random_function(max_x,max_y)
        vals.add(v[(max_x,max_y)])
    return sorted(vals)

print(sample_values(4,4, trials=200)[:20])

def compute_min_max(max_x, max_y):
    # exhaustive search with pruning using constraints, but may be large.
    # We'll use recursion and keep track of min/max for each cell.
    min_vals = {}
    max_vals = {}
    # We'll store for each cell the set of possible values encountered.
    possible = {}
    # Use backtracking similar to earlier but store values at each cell.
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            # record values
            for cell,val in values.items():
                possible.setdefault(cell,set()).add(val)
            return
        cell = cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible_vals=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            # bound range maybe -10..10
            possible_vals=set(range(-10,11))
        for val in possible_vals:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    # compute min max
    mins={cell:min(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    maxs={cell:max(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    return mins, maxs

mins,maxs=compute_min_max(3,3)
print("min at (3,3):", mins[(3,3)], "max:", maxs[(3,3)])

def compute_min_max_bound(max_x, max_y, bound):
    possible = {}
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            for cell,val in values.items():
                possible.setdefault(cell,set()).add(val)
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible_vals=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible_vals=set(range(-bound,bound+1))
        for val in possible_vals:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    mins={cell:min(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    maxs={cell:max(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    return mins, maxs

mins,maxs=compute_min_max_bound(3,3, bound=30)
print(mins[(3,3)], maxs[(3,3)])

def compute_min_max_bound(max_x, max_y, bound):
    possible = {}
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            for cell,val in values.items():
                possible.setdefault(cell,set()).add(val)
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible_vals=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible_vals=set(range(-bound,bound+1))
        for val in possible_vals:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    mins={cell:min(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    maxs={cell:max(vals) for cell,vals in possible.items()}
    return mins, maxs

mins,maxs=compute_min_max_bound(3,3, bound=30)
print(mins[(3,3)], maxs[(3,3)])

def max_value_nonnegative(max_x, max_y, bound):
    # returns max possible value at (max_x,max_y) with all values >=0
    best = -1
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn = min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        nonlocal best
        if idx==len(cells):
            val = values[(max_x,max_y)]
            if val>best:
                best=val
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible=set(range(0,bound+1))  # non-negative only
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            # check non-negativity for all assigned (already ensured)
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return best

print(max_value_nonnegative(2,2,bound=20))

ex = { (0,0):0, (0,1):1, (0,2):2, (0,3):0, (1,0):2, (1,1):0, (1,2):1, (1,3):0, (2,0):1, (2,1):2, (2,2):0, (2,3):0}
def check_all(vals, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            cells = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
            if all(c in vals for c in cells):
                a,b,c = [vals[c] for c in cells]
                mn=min(a,b,c)
                if sorted([a,b,c]) != [mn,mn+1,mn+2]:
                    return False
    return True

def find_bad(vals, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            cells = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
            if all(c in vals for c in cells):
                a,b,c = [vals[c] for c in cells]
                mn=min(a,b,c)
                if sorted([a,b,c]) != [mn,mn+1,mn+2]:

    return None

def min_value_nonnegative(max_x, max_y, bound):
    best = None
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn=min(vals)
            if sorted(vals) != [mn,mn+1,mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        nonlocal best
        if idx==len(cells):
            val = values[(max_x,max_y)]
            if best is None or val<best:
                best=val
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible=set(range(0,bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return best

def find_example_target(max_x,max_y,target,bound):
    values = {(0,0):0}
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell = {}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals = [values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn=min(vals)
            if sorted(vals)!=[mn,mn+1,mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    solution=None
    def backtrack(idx):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution is not None:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            if values[target]==0:
                solution=dict(values)
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible=set(range(0,bound+1))
        for val in possible:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return solution

sol = find_example_target(2,2,(2,2),bound=10)
print(sol)

def verify_grid(vals, max_x, max_y):
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            cells = [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
            if all(c in vals for c in cells):
                a,b,c = [vals[c] for c in cells]
                mn=min(a,b,c)
                if sorted([a,b,c]) != [mn,mn+1,mn+2]:
                    return False, (x,y)
    return True, None

def possible_target_nonnegative(max_x, max_y, target, bound):
    # returns set of possible values at target with non-negative values up to bound
    possible=set()
    values={(0,0):0}
    cells=[(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1) if not (x==0 and y==0)]
    squares_by_cell={}
    for x in range(max_x):
        for y in range(max_y):
            for cell in [(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]:
                squares_by_cell.setdefault(cell, []).append((x,y))
    def check_square(x,y):
        cells_sq=[(x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)]
        if all(c in values for c in cells_sq):
            vals=[values[c] for c in cells_sq]
            mn=min(vals)
            if sorted(vals)!=[mn,mn+1,mn+2]:
                return False
        return True
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx==len(cells):
            possible.add(values[target])
            return
        cell=cells[idx]
        constraints=[]
        for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
            x0,y0=sq
            other=[(x0,y0),(x0,y0+1),(x0+1,y0)]
            other.remove(cell)
            if all(o in values for o in other):
                a,b=values[other[0]], values[other[1]]
                mn=min(a,b); mx=max(a,b)
                if mx-mn==2:
                    constraints.append({mn+1})
                elif mx-mn==1:
                    cand={mn-1,mx+1}
                    cand={c for c in cand if c not in (a,b)}
                    constraints.append(cand)
                else:
                    return
        if constraints:
            possible_vals=set.intersection(*constraints)
        else:
            possible_vals=set(range(0,bound+1))
        for val in possible_vals:
            values[cell]=val
            ok=True
            for sq in squares_by_cell.get(cell, []):
                if not check_square(*sq):
                    ok=False
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1)
            del values[cell]
    backtrack(0)
    return possible

print(possible_target_nonnegative(2,2,(2,2), bound=5))

[CODE] (turn 1):
def check_linear(p,q,r,limit=5):
    for x in range(limit):
        for y in range(limit):
            a = p*x + q*y + r
            b = p*x + q*(y+1) + r
            c = p*(x+1) + q*y + r
            s = sorted([a,b,c])
            if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len(set([a,b,c]))!=3:
                return False
    return True

for p,q in [(1,2),(2,1),(-1,-2),(-2,-1)]:
    print(p,q, check_linear(p,q,0))

def check_linear(p,q,r,limit=5):
    for x in range(limit):
        for y in range(limit):
            a = p*x + q*y + r
            b = p*x + q*(y+1) + r
            c = p*(x+1) + q*y + r
            s = sorted([a,b,c])
            if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len(set([a,b,c]))!=3:
                return False
    return True

from functools import lru_cache

def possible_values(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size (max_x+1) x (max_y+1) inclusive indices 0..max_x,0..max_y
    # returns set of possible values at (max_x, max_y)
    # using backtracking
    from collections import defaultdict
    
    # We'll store values in dict (x,y)->value
    # We'll assign cells in order (row major)
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    
    results = set()
    
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            results.add(grid[(max_x,max_y)])
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values for this cell based on constraints with already assigned neighbors.
        # Since values are natural numbers, we could bound them. For small grid we can try range 0..5 maybe.
        # But we need to know possible range. Let's try up to 5 for now.
        for val in range(0,6):
            # assign
            grid[(x,y)] = val
            # check any corner constraints that are now fully assigned
            ok = True
            # corners that involve (x,y) as one of the three cells:
            # corner (x,y) uses (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)
            # corner (x-1,y) uses (x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)
            # corner (x,y-1) uses (x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)
            # need to ensure indices within bounds
            corners = []
            if x <= max_x-1 and y <= max_y-1:
                corners.append((x,y))
            if x-1 >=0 and y <= max_y-1:
                corners.append((x-1,y))
            if x <= max_x-1 and y-1 >=0:
                corners.append((x,y-1))
            for cx,cy in corners:
                # check if the three cells are assigned
                cells_needed = [(cx,cy), (cx,cy+1), (cx+1,cy)]
                if all(c in grid for c in cells_needed):
                    a,b,c = [grid[c] for c in cells_needed]
                    s = sorted([a,b,c])
                    if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a,b,c})!=3:
                        ok=False
                        break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            # remove assignment
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return results

print(possible_values(2,2))

def possible_values(max_x, max_y):
    # grid size (max_x+1) x (max_y+1) inclusive indices 0..max_x,0..max_y
    # returns set of possible values at (max_x, max_y)
    # using backtracking
    from collections import defaultdict
    
    # We'll store values in dict (x,y)->value
    # We'll assign cells in order (row major)
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    
    results = set()
    
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            results.add(grid[(max_x,max_y)])
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values for this cell based on constraints with already assigned neighbors.
        # Since values are natural numbers, we could bound them. For small grid we can try range 0..5 maybe.
        # But we need to know possible range. Let's try up to 5 for now.
        for val in range(0,6):
            # assign
            grid[(x,y)] = val
            # check any corner constraints that are now fully assigned
            ok = True
            # corners that involve (x,y) as one of the three cells:
            # corner (x,y) uses (x,y),(x,y+1),(x+1,y)
            # corner (x-1,y) uses (x-1,y),(x-1,y+1),(x,y)
            # corner (x,y-1) uses (x,y-1),(x,y),(x+1,y-1)
            # need to ensure indices within bounds
            corners = []
            if x <= max_x-1 and y <= max_y-1:
                corners.append((x,y))
            if x-1 >=0 and y <= max_y-1:
                corners.append((x-1,y))
            if x <= max_x-1 and y-1 >=0:
                corners.append((x,y-1))
            for cx,cy in corners:
                # check if the three cells are assigned
                cells_needed = [(cx,cy), (cx,cy+1), (cx+1,cy)]
                if all(c in grid for c in cells_needed):
                    a,b,c = [grid[c] for c in cells_needed]
                    s = sorted([a,b,c])
                    if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a,b,c})!=3:
                        ok=False
                        break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            # remove assignment
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})

def possible_values_range(max_x,max_y, val_max):
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    results=set()
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            results.add(grid[(max_x,max_y)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        for val in range(val_max+1):
            grid[(x,y)]=val
            ok=True
            corners=[]
            if x<=max_x-1 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x,y))
            if x-1>=0 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x-1,y))
            if x<=max_x-1 and y-1>=0:
                corners.append((x,y-1))
            for cx,cy in corners:
                cells_needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                if all(c in grid for c in cells_needed):
                    a,b,c=[grid[c] for c in cells_needed]
                    s=sorted([a,b,c])
                    if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a,b,c})!=3:
                        ok=False
                        break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return results

[CODE] (turn 7):
def find_grid(max_x,max_y,target):
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    solution=None
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution is not None:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            if grid[(max_x,max_y)]==target:
                solution=grid.copy()
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        for val in range(0,6):
            grid[(x,y)]=val
            ok=True
            corners=[]
            if x<=max_x-1 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x,y))
            if x-1>=0 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x-1,y))
            if x<=max_x-1 and y-1>=0:
                corners.append((x,y-1))
            for cx,cy in corners:
                cells_needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                if all(c in grid for c in cells_needed):
                    a,b,c=[grid[c] for c in cells_needed]
                    s=sorted([a,b,c])
                    if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a,b,c})!=3:
                        ok=False
                        break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return solution

sol=find_grid(2,2,0)
sol

def find_grid_range(max_x,max_y,target,val_max):
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    solution=None
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution is not None:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            if grid[(max_x,max_y)]==target:
                solution=grid.copy()
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        for val in range(val_max+1):
            grid[(x,y)]=val
            ok=True
            corners=[]
            if x<=max_x-1 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x,y))
            if x-1>=0 and y<=max_y-1:
                corners.append((x-1,y))
            if x<=max_x-1 and y-1>=0:
                corners.append((x,y-1))
            for cx,cy in corners:
                cells_needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                if all(c in grid for c in cells_needed):
                    a,b,c=[grid[c] for c in cells_needed]
                    s=sorted([a,b,c])
                    if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a,b,c})!=3:
                        ok=False
                        break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return solution

sol12=find_grid_range(2,2,12,12)
print(sol12)

[CODE] (turn 13):
def exists_solution(a,b,target,val_max=20):
    # grid indices 0..a+1, 0..b+1 inclusive (to include right/up neighbors of target)
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    # we will enforce g(0,0)=0 and g(a,b)=target
    # backtracking
    solution=False
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            solution=True
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        # if fixed values:
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        elif (x,y)==(a,b):
            vals=[target]
        else:
            vals=range(val_max+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check any corner whose three cells are assigned
            # corners (cx,cy) with cx<=max_x-1, cy<=max_y-1
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return solution

for N in range(0,13):
    print(N, exists_solution(2,2,N, val_max=15))

[CODE] (turn 13):
def exists_solution(a,b,target,val_max=20):
    # grid indices 0..a+1, 0..b+1 inclusive (to include right/up neighbors of target)
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    # we will enforce g(0,0)=0 and g(a,b)=target
    # backtracking
    solution=False
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            solution=True
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        # if fixed values:
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        elif (x,y)==(a,b):
            vals=[target]
        else:
            vals=range(val_max+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check any corner whose three cells are assigned
            # corners (cx,cy) with cx<=max_x-1, cy<=max_y-1
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return solution

for N in range(0,13):

[CODE] (turn 14):
def reachable_set(a,b,limit=30):
    # search for values up to limit using backtracking with extra row/col
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    reachable=set()
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            reachable.add(grid[(a,b)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        else:
            # bound values up to limit
            vals=range(limit+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check corners where all three assigned
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return reachable

print(reachable_set(1,1,limit=12))

[CODE] (turn 14):
def reachable_set(a,b,limit=30):
    # search for values up to limit using backtracking with extra row/col
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    reachable=set()
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            reachable.add(grid[(a,b)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        else:
            # bound values up to limit
            vals=range(limit+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check corners where all three assigned
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return reachable

def find_solution(a,b,target,val_max=10):
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    solution=None
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        nonlocal solution
        if solution is not None:
            return
        if idx==len(cells):
            if grid[(a,b)]==target:
                solution=grid.copy()
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        elif (x,y)==(a,b):
            vals=[target]
        else:
            vals=range(val_max+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return solution

sol=find_solution(2,0,0, val_max=5)
print(sol)

from functools import lru_cache

def reachable_set(a,b,limit=10):
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    reachable=set()
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            reachable.add(grid[(a,b)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        else:
            vals=range(limit+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            # check any corner where all three assigned
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return reachable

for a in range(0,4):
    for b in range(0,4):
        if a==0 and b==0: continue
        print((a,b), sorted(reachable_set(a,b,limit=8)))

def reachable_set(a,b,limit=12):
    max_x=a+1
    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    reachable=set()
    # Use recursion with pruning; limit small
    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            reachable.add(grid[(a,b)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        else:
            vals=range(limit+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])
                        if s[2]-s[0]!=2 or len({a1,b1,c1})!=3:
                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return reachable

def compute(a,b):
    s=reachable_set(a,b,limit=12)
    return sorted(s), sorted({v%3 for v in s})

def reachable_set(a,b,limit=12):

    max_y=b+1
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]
    reachable=set()

    def backtrack(idx,grid):
        if idx==len(cells):
            reachable.add(grid[(a,b)])
            return
        x,y=cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            vals=[0]
        else:
            vals=range(limit+1)
        for v in vals:
            grid[(x,y)]=v
            ok=True
            for cx in range(max_x):
                for cy in range(max_y):
                    needed=[(cx,cy),(cx,cy+1),(cx+1,cy)]
                    if all(p in grid for p in needed):
                        a1,b1,c1=[grid[p] for p in needed]
                        s=sorted([a1,b1,c1])

                            ok=False
                            break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                backtrack(idx+1,grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0,{})
    return reachable

For (1,1): a+2b=1+2=3 ≡0 mod3. Between 0 and 2(a+b)=4 => {0,3}. matches.

For (1,2): a+2b=1+4=5 ≡2 mod3. Between 0 and 2(a+b)=6 => numbers ≡2 mod3: {2,5}. But reachable set we observed {1,2,4,5}. So includes also numbers ≡1 mod3. So pattern not just that.

Maybe reachable set consists of numbers congruent to (a+b) mod 1? Not.

Thus maybe reachable residues are those not equal to (a+2b) mod 3? Not.

Let's compute residues for (2,1): a+2b=2+2=4 ≡1. reachable {1,2,4,5} residues 1,2,1,2. So includes residues 1 and 2, not 0. So pattern: when a,b both non-zero, reachable residues are those not equal to (a+2b) mod 3? For (2,2) a+2b=0, reachable residues 0 only. So not.

Thus pattern: If a+b is odd? Let's check: (1,2) sum=3 odd, residues 1 and 2. (2,1) sum=3 odd. (2,3) sum=5 odd. (1,1) sum=2 even gave only 0. (2,2) sum=4 even gave only 0. (3,3) sum=6 even gave only 0. (0,2) sum=2 even gave residues 0,1,2 (all). But axis case is special.

Thus maybe for interior points (both coordinates >0), if a+b is even, reachable residues only 0 mod3; if a+b is odd, reachable residues are 1 and 2 mod3 (i.e., not 0). Let's test: (1,1) even sum 2 -> residues 0 only (true). (2,2) sum 4 even -> residues 0 only (true). (3,3) sum 6 even -> residues 0 only (true). (1,2) sum 3 odd -> residues 1 and 2 (true). Thus rule: For a,b>0, parity of a+b determines which residues modulo 3 are possible: if a+b even, only multiples of 3; if odd, only numbers not divisible by 3.

For (2,1), max = 2a+b=5 matches reachable max 5. For (2,2), both equal 6.

Thus maximum reachable value = a+2b if a <= b? Actually whichever larger. So max = a+2b if b >= a, else 2a+b. So max = a+2b when b >= a else 2a+b. That's equal to a+2b + (a-b) if a>b? Not needed.

Thus max = a+2b if b>=a else 2a+b = a+2b + (a-b) if a>b.

def generate_grid(max_x, max_y):
    # returns list of possible assignments dict
    assignments = []
    # order of cells: row-major
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # we will use recursion
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            assignments.append(dict(grid))
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values: based on neighbors already assigned?
        # Since increments are 1 or 2, we can bound possible values.
        # Let's compute min and max possible based on known neighbors.
        # For each neighbor left (x-1,y) or down (x,y-1) that is assigned,
        # the value must differ by 1 or 2.
        possible = set()
        # We'll try values from 0 to 2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        max_val = 2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        for val in range(max_val+1):
            ok = True
            # check left neighbor if assigned
            if (x-1,y) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x-1,y)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if (x,y-1) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x,y-1)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if not ok:
                continue
            # also check triple condition for any (i,j) where this cell completes the triple
            # triples: (x,y) could be (i,j), (i,j+1), or (i+1,j)
            # We'll check all triples that involve (x,y) and have other two cells assigned.
            # For each such triple, verify consecutive condition.
            # define helper:
            def check_triple(a,b,c):
                vals = [grid.get(a), grid.get(b), grid.get(c)]
                # replace None with val if it's the current cell
                # but we need to know which is current cell
                # We'll call with actual values
                pass
            # We'll just manually check each possible triple:
            # case 1: (x,y) as (i,j)
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case 2: (x,y) as (i,j+1) => then triple is (i,j), (i,j+1), (i+1,j)
            # So (i,j) = (x, y-1), (i+1,j) = (x+1, y-1)
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case 3: (x,y) as (i+1,j) => triple (i,j), (i,j+1), (i+1,j)
            # So (i,j) = (x-1, y), (i,j+1) = (x-1, y+1)
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                possible.add(val)
        # if no possible values, backtrack fails
        for val in possible:
            grid[(x,y)] = val
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return assignments

assignments = generate_grid(2,2)
len(assignments)

[CODE] (turn 2):
def generate_grid(max_x, max_y):
    # returns list of possible assignments dict
    assignments = []
    # order of cells: row-major
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # we will use recursion
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            assignments.append(dict(grid))
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        # Determine possible values: based on neighbors already assigned?
        # Since increments are 1 or 2, we can bound possible values.
        # Let's compute min and max possible based on known neighbors.
        # For each neighbor left (x-1,y) or down (x,y-1) that is assigned,
        # the value must differ by 1 or 2.
        possible = set()
        # We'll try values from 0 to 2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        max_val = 2*(max_x+max_y)+2
        for val in range(max_val+1):
            ok = True
            # check left neighbor if assigned
            if (x-1,y) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x-1,y)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if (x,y-1) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x,y-1)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if not ok:
                continue
            # also check triple condition for any (i,j) where this cell completes the triple
            # triples: (x,y) could be (i,j), (i,j+1), or (i+1,j)
            # We'll check all triples that involve (x,y) and have other two cells assigned.
            # For each such triple, verify consecutive condition.
            # define helper:
            def check_triple(a,b,c):
                vals = [grid.get(a), grid.get(b), grid.get(c)]
                # replace None with val if it's the current cell
                # but we need to know which is current cell
                # We'll call with actual values
                pass
            # We'll just manually check each possible triple:
            # case 1: (x,y) as (i,j)
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case 2: (x,y) as (i,j+1) => then triple is (i,j), (i,j+1), (i+1,j)
            # So (i,j) = (x, y-1), (i+1,j) = (x+1, y-1)
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case 3: (x,y) as (i+1,j) => triple (i,j), (i,j+1), (i+1,j)
            # So (i,j) = (x-1, y), (i,j+1) = (x-1, y+1)
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                possible.add(val)
        # if no possible values, backtrack fails
        for val in possible:
            grid[(x,y)] = val
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return assignments

assignments = generate_grid(2,2)
len(assignments)

[CODE] (turn 3):
def possible_values(max_x, max_y):
    assignments = generate_grid(max_x, max_y)
    # compute set of values at each cell
    cell_vals = {}
    for (x,y) in [(i,j) for i in range(max_x+1) for j in range(max_y+1)]:
        cell_vals[(x,y)] = set()
    for a in assignments:
        for (x,y),v in a.items():
            cell_vals[(x,y)].add(v)
    return cell_vals

[CODE] (turn 4):
def has_decrease(assign):
    for (x,y),v in assign.items():
        if (x+1,y) in assign:
            if assign[(x+1,y)] < v:
                return True
        if (x,y+1) in assign:
            if assign[(x,y+1)] < v:
                return True
    return False

assignments = generate_grid(2,2)
any(has_decrease(a) for a in assignments)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def generate_grid_fixed(max_x, max_y):
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # ensure (0,0) first
    cells.sort(key=lambda p: (p[0]!=0 or p[1]!=0, p[0], p[1]))
    assignments = []
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            assignments.append(dict(grid))
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            grid[(0,0)] = 0
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(0,0)]
            return
        # possible values bound: maybe up to 2*(max_x+max_y)+5
        max_val = 2*(max_x+max_y)+5
        possible = []
        for val in range(max_val+1):
            ok=True
            # check diff with left and down if assigned
            if (x-1,y) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x-1,y)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if (x,y-1) in grid:
                diff = abs(val - grid[(x,y-1)])
                if diff not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if not ok:
                continue
            # check triples where this cell completes
            # case (x,y) as (i,j)
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i,j+1)
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i+1,j)
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                possible.append(val)
        for val in possible:
            grid[(x,y)] = val
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return assignments

def residue_by_mod2(max_n):
    assign = generate_grid_fixed(max_n, max_n)
    cell_vals = {}
    for a in assign:
        for (x,y),v in a.items():
            cell_vals.setdefault((x,y), set()).add(v)
    table = {}
    for (x,y),vals in cell_vals.items():
        table[(x%2, y%2)] = table.get((x%2, y%2), set()).union({v%3 for v in vals})
    return table

residue_by_mod2(3)

[CODE] (turn 26):
def residue_pattern(max_n):
    assign = generate_grid_fixed(max_n, max_n)
    cell_vals = {}
    for a in assign:
        for (x,y),v in a.items():
            cell_vals.setdefault((x,y), set()).add(v%3)
    pattern = {}
    for (x,y),res_set in cell_vals.items():
        pattern[(x%3, y%3)] = pattern.get((x%3, y%3), set()).union(res_set)
    return pattern

pattern = residue_pattern(5)
pattern

[CODE] (turn 30):
def generate_colorings(max_x, max_y):
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # ensure (0,0)=0 (since we can fix color by global permutation)
    # We'll fix c(0,0)=0 to reduce symmetries.
    assignments = []
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            assignments.append(dict(grid))
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            grid[(0,0)] = 0
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(0,0)]
            return
        possible = [0,1,2]
        for val in possible:
            ok=True
            # check L-shape triples where this cell completes
            # same as before but only modulo 3 distinctness condition
            # triple must have three distinct colors
            # case (x,y) as (i,j)
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i,j+1)
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i+1,j)
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                grid[(x,y)] = val
                backtrack(idx+1, grid)
                del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return assignments

colorings4 = generate_colorings(3,3)
len(colorings4)

[CODE] (turn 30):
def generate_colorings(max_x, max_y):
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    # ensure (0,0)=0 (since we can fix color by global permutation)
    # We'll fix c(0,0)=0 to reduce symmetries.
    assignments = []
    def backtrack(idx, grid):
        if idx == len(cells):
            assignments.append(dict(grid))
            return
        x,y = cells[idx]
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            grid[(0,0)] = 0
            backtrack(idx+1, grid)
            del grid[(0,0)]
            return
        possible = [0,1,2]
        for val in possible:
            ok=True
            # check L-shape triples where this cell completes
            # same as before but only modulo 3 distinctness condition
            # triple must have three distinct colors
            # case (x,y) as (i,j)
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i,j+1)
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            # case as (i+1,j)
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                if len(set(vals)) != 3:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                grid[(x,y)] = val
                backtrack(idx+1, grid)
                del grid[(x,y)]
    backtrack(0, {})
    return assignments

[CODE] (turn 33):
def random_solution(max_x, max_y, seed=0):
    import random
    random.seed(seed)
    cells = [(x,y) for x in range(max_x+1) for y in range(max_y+1)]
    cells.sort(key=lambda p: (p[0]+p[1], p[0]))  # maybe BFS order
    grid = {(0,0):0}
    for (x,y) in cells:
        if (x,y)==(0,0):
            continue
        # compute possible values based on constraints (full integer values, not just modulo)
        # We'll compute possible values up to some bound.
        possible = []
        max_val = 2*(max_x+max_y)+5
        for val in range(max_val+1):
            ok=True
            # diff with left and down
            if (x-1,y) in grid:
                if abs(val - grid[(x-1,y)]) not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if (x,y-1) in grid:
                if abs(val - grid[(x,y-1)]) not in (1,2):
                    ok=False
            if not ok:
                continue
            # triple checks
            if (x, y+1) in grid and (x+1, y) in grid:
                vals = [val, grid[(x, y+1)], grid[(x+1, y)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok and (x, y-1) in grid and (x+1, y-1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x, y-1)], val, grid[(x+1, y-1)]]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok and (x-1, y) in grid and (x-1, y+1) in grid:
                vals = [grid[(x-1, y)], grid[(x-1, y+1)], val]
                mn = min(vals)
                if sorted(vals) != [mn, mn+1, mn+2]:
                    ok=False
            if ok:
                possible.append(val)
        if not possible:
            return None  # dead end
        # choose random
        grid[(x,y)] = random.choice(possible)
    return grid

sol = random_solution(5,5,seed=1)
sol is not None

[CODE] (turn 34):
def residues_match(grid, pattern_func):
    for (x,y),v in grid.items():
        if v % 3 != pattern_func(x,y):
            return False
    return True

def matches_any(grid):
    return residues_match(grid, lambda x,y: (x+2*y)%3) or residues_match(grid, lambda x,y: (2*x+y)%3)
